# Upstream sync ledger

Updated: 2026-10-28 (Asia/Shanghai). This is the checkpoint for selective backports from `router-for-me/CLIProxyAPI` into `vpromise/CodexProxy`.

**The reviewed upstream boundary is not a full merge boundary.** Local implementation and publication are recorded separately below. Only the selected behavior has been integrated in the indicated scope. Original upstream commits may remain outside this fork's ancestry, so GitHub's behind count is not a count of missing fixes. Published source and server deployment are separate states.

## Deployed to dmit2 — 2026-10-23

At **2026-10-23 15:30:53 Asia/Shanghai**, dmit2's native `codexproxy.service` was updated from `baabdc29ad44cf7e30286cfc8078dc7cf84a09d7` to published main **[`a20079c2`](https://github.com/vpromise/CodexProxy/commit/a20079c2d233f0a6e7040e76fad6effa2e7407cf)**. Verification completed at **15:33:48 Asia/Shanghai**. This rollout includes CodexProxy branding and the new logo (`04f0bc77`), revoked client-key record removal (`a181bc25`), the October 23 protocol/WebSocket fixes (`55d11ada`), and the previously deployed memory-retention repair (`baabdc29`, now merged into main). No config/schema migration was needed. dmit and bawg were outside this rollout.

The binary was cross-built with Go 1.27.1, `CGO_ENABLED=0`, `-mod=readonly`, `-trimpath`, `-buildvcs=false`, and explicit version/commit/date metadata. The clean detached source matched the previously validated branding tree and its manifest before and after the build. Reused validation covers 369 frontend tests plus lint/type checks/production build, management-asset tests, the host build, and the preceding main integration's 68-package/3975-test Go suite and 5-package/450-test race suite. The two documented macOS loopback-binding exclusions remain explicit; this deployment did not rerun Linux unit tests. Frozen model catalogs and the checked-in management bundle were retained.

The installed executable and `/proc/2105531/exe` both match the artifact hash. Fifteen HTTP gates passed on both the old and new versions across loopback, the nginx origin route, and public HTTPS: health 200, hello HEAD 204, unauthenticated models/config 401, expected version/commit headers, and exact management HTML. Five independent public checks from the operator workstation also passed with normal TLS validation. The existing origin-certificate exception was limited to the loopback gateway probe. An existing client key remained server-side and was used only for read-only `/v1/models`, which returned 200 with 15 model entries. No inference, credential rotation/refresh, or management write was initiated. Authenticated management checks were unavailable because only the configured management-key hash was present.

The current production configuration, binary ownership/mode, systemd unit/drop-in bytes, normalized process environment and neighboring new-api/nginx/s-ui identities were preserved. The inactive Docker service remained inactive. **MemoryMax 1 GiB, MemoryHigh 896 MiB, MemorySwapMax 256 MiB, CPUQuota 150%, GOMEMLIMIT 512 MiB**, and restart protection are unchanged. Existing logging settings remain commercial mode enabled, file logging enabled, detailed request logging disabled, usage statistics enabled, and a 128 MiB log-retention cap. No account/configuration data was rewritten or restored.

A complete two-minute observation recorded 24 healthy samples, zero automatic restarts, and no memory-high/max/OOM events. Startup journal and recent application log checks found zero error/fatal/panic records; intentional unauthenticated probes were classified separately. Final service cgroup memory was approximately 22.4 MiB. These checks establish deployment readiness and short observation stability, not equal-load memory savings or end-to-end provider correctness.

Release-local `deployment.json`, `FINAL_VERIFICATION.json`, `EXTERNAL_PUBLIC.json`, and root `LATEST_DEPLOYMENT.json` were written and read back. Existing resource/logging tuning history remains in the latest record. The release retains the exact source archive/manifest, build metadata, previous executable/config/unit backups, and a syntax-checked **`ROLLBACK.sh`**. That script restores only the preceding executable and restarts CodexProxy, verifies recovery, and supersedes the latest receipt; it preserves current account data, configuration and resource limits. This bookkeeping follow-up does not change the running source SHA.

## Memory repair integrated into main — 2026-10-23

The user-authorized merge integrates published `codex/memory-retention-fix`
`c09004149902be4f5212e3768d88e1939256c6b1`, including implementation
[baabdc29](https://github.com/vpromise/CodexProxy/commit/baabdc29ad44cf7e30286cfc8078dc7cf84a09d7),
into main based on `4c479da8d351b142def19c01a36574e63dc60c29`. It preserves the
October 23 upstream repairs (`55d11ada`) and revoked-key record cleanup
(`a181bc25`). Both memory cleanup code paths and their regression files match
the original repair exactly; the automatic merge had no conflicts.

Consumed usage queue slots release completed request contexts. Request logging
finalization releases deferred upstream request builders after diagnostics have
finished, retaining retry details until error logging completes. No scheduling,
isolation policy, provider protocol, active-request budget or timeout change is
introduced by this merge.

Validation on Go 1.27.1, darwin/arm64: full Go tests passed **68 packages / 3975
top-level tests**; exactly the two preexisting 127.0.0.2 binding tests were excluded
after a fresh local bind probe reproduced errno 49. The optional external TLS
capture test skipped because it was unconfigured. Targeted race suites passed
**5 packages / 450 top-level tests** across middleware, usage, API and HTTP/WS
handlers. Three allocation-only tests intentionally skip under race and passed
in the ordinary suite. The server build, formatting and diff checks passed.
All tests used fixtures; no real-account inference or server access occurred.

An initial result-summary script failed after the tests completed; the corrected
collector reran both suites and recorded their exit status as zero. Final full
and race output SHA-256 values are
`def6b25fa9551928b2690d79f3b3e3bbbcc2db727653f09a55cc8985d5b43cf5` and
`540db7a764c176717b9b3da4dde23024ce2646d45cb3723a6ea6aff45f271a1f`.
The main management assets and the previous key-cleanup changes are unchanged.

This source merge supersedes earlier statements that the memory branch is
outside main. It performs no deployment or resource/logging configuration change.
The October 22 recovery limits below are historical values from that rollout,
not a readback of current server limits. Existing local deployment-ledger edits
in the user's original checkout were preserved byte for byte.

## dmit2 memory incident recovery — 2026-10-22

At **2026-10-22 21:21:19 Asia/Shanghai**, dmit2 was restored to active/running and enabled from the verified native systemd release **[baabdc29](https://github.com/vpromise/CodexProxy/commit/baabdc29ad44cf7e30286cfc8078dc7cf84a09d7)** on **`codex/memory-retention-fix`**. This entry supersedes earlier dmit2 availability/version statements below. Main, dmit and bawg were not changed by this rollout. The implementation is based on merged main `ce1b7cd77d7e7d51b4580f44caa4684611c468d6`; the normal feature-branch push and exact remote SHA were verified. A later ledger-only commit does not change the running source SHA.

The prior `10f500fe2540d055ed1e79e7bbf239bddcaf1963` native service had been deliberately stopped and disabled after five kernel-confirmed OOM kills. The replacement clears consumed usage queue slots and deferred diagnostic capture builders after final logging. Tests prove completed-request collection without losing multi-attempt error logs; this does not eliminate active-request body copies or establish the sole cause of the production OOMs.

Validation: before/after weak-reference and native middleware regressions, targeted race tests, host and static Linux builds passed. All 68 packages passed on macOS with exactly two preexisting `127.0.0.2` binding failures excluded; both failures passed in the complete `helps` package suite on Linux. Complete Linux usage and middleware suites also passed. Four sustained local scenarios completed 2,569 synthetic requests; three Linux scenarios completed 362 requests. Normal responses, synthetic upstream errors and client cancellation were exercised with fake credentials, loopback-only network and no access to production config/auth. At quiescent forced-GC checkpoints, incremental live heap remained within 0.25 MiB. No forced GC ran during traffic. Linux cgroup peaks were 395.5 MiB (8 MiB × 4), 670.8 MiB (8 MiB × 8) and 717.7 MiB (16 MiB × 4), with no OOM events. These are bounded synthetic scenarios, not a claim of production capacity or a measured reduction in active-request peak memory.

Only `codexproxy.service` gained a dedicated `90-memory-protection.conf`: `MemoryMax=768M`, `MemorySwapMax=128M`, `CPUQuota=100%` (one CPU on this two-CPU host), `StartLimitIntervalSec=300`, `StartLimitBurst=3`, `RestartSec=15s`, CPU/memory accounting enabled. Existing `GOMEMLIMIT=512MiB` and production configuration were preserved. `MemoryHigh` remains unlimited; the Go soft limit and cgroup hard backstop serve different purposes. A larger live workload may reach the service cap; containment is not proof that all memory optimizations are complete.

The installed file and `/proc/<pid>/exe` both matched the static Go 1.27.1 Linux/amd64 artifact. Fifteen HTTP contracts passed across loopback, local gateway and public HTTPS: health 200, hello HEAD 204, unauthenticated models/config 401, expected version/commit, and exact management HTML. Public TLS was validated normally; only the existing loopback origin-certificate probe used the established local exception. An existing API key was used server-side solely for read-only `/v1/models` (200), never exported. No real inference or credential-refresh test was initiated. After at least 120 seconds of observation, there were zero restarts/OOMs and no startup error/panic records. Final process RSS was 59.8 MiB and service cgroup memory was 28.2 MiB, host available memory 1503 MiB; these low-load observations do not represent real inference memory. new-api, s-ui and nginx retained their original PIDs, start times and restart counts.

- Recovery: run that release's syntax-checked `ROLLBACK.sh`; it restores the old executable and the **stopped/disabled incident baseline**, removes only the owned protection drop-in, and does not overwrite account data or restart the former OOM-prone binary.

Production configurations and raw logs remain in the mode-0700 release directory. The first preparation extraction raced an unfinished upload and failed before activation; after transfer completion all payload hashes and the safe isolated help smoke passed. No bypass of a failed runtime gate was used. Next validation is observation of user-initiated real traffic; no unbounded background load or profiling listener remains.

The isolated candidate starts at verified fork main **7ce52cceec8c2730eb7af845afdc9b40ec6573b9** (tree **a14d108a6c3882b8b10dc7985362b243d7b82699**). Existing feature worktrees contain no uncommitted code or uncovered authorized publication batch.

### Selected behavior and local boundaries

1. **6b56ce6c, partial:** use a literal-path existence check before supported Codex/Responses and Claude summary deletions. Missing fields no longer copy the full request. Existing present-field output, explicit-null handling, canonical thinking and provider filtering are preserved. Gemini/Antigravity appliers are excluded. A 1 MiB missing-summary request allocated six times on the old code and passes the at-most-one-allocation regression after adaptation.
2. **b7442218, partial:** snapshot the usage API key, including an authoritative empty key, while the HTTP handler owns its Gin context; keep the snapshot through direct SDK execution. Home updates only the existing usage holder, and each queued record gets a detached identity. Reused contexts cannot attribute late usage to the next caller. Old code deterministically returned `next-client` for both an original key and a missing original key. The fork has no upstream SIWC/client-detection or AccessProvider/IsNativeKey usage fields, so those changes are not imported. Account access grants, provider pools, session UUIDs, token selection, retry/cooldown policy and queue-slot release are unchanged.
3. **ad8bdd3f, bounded objective already present:** no production log-decoder change. The fork already caps output at 32 MiB, bounds decoder memory/window, uses low-memory decoding and limits encoding layers to four. Added tests exercise a 40 MiB expansion, oversized-window raw fallback and byte-for-byte restoration of the inference wire body. The local limit-derived window cap remains; it is not claimed identical to upstream's fixed 8 MiB window. This is not an active-request admission limit or a complete fix for the separate incomplete-body descriptor issue.

### Selected behavior and preservation

- Handler bootstrap now uses the already-accepted cancellation-aware reader. It does not change credential cancellation classification, bootstrap retry policy or queued terminal-error precedence. The upstream test's assumption that cancellation can never be a startup 499 does not hold locally: the first baseline/focused failures at that assertion were fixture incompatibility, **not proof of this bug**. The corrected control accepts only a context-canceled 499, retains the canceled-completion assertion, and passes on old and new source. A separate filtered-bootstrap fixture cancels after auth bootstrap and drops the only payload; the old handler reported `succeeded / 200` at round1, while the adapted handler reports canceled across 2000 rounds. Both tests run in the full/race suites.
- Claude reasoning usage appears in stream delta, trailing usage and non-stream output only when a valid nonnegative int64 counter is reported. Explicit zero remains present; missing/null/string/negative/fractional/overflow values do not invent a false zero or replace prior valid stream usage. Reasoning remains a subset of output, not an extra total. Cache read/write, completion and total accounting are preserved. Four upstream reasoning assertions fail on old source; final tests also cover malformed counters and cache/stream-state preservation.
- CORS adaptation retains the fork's shared `setCORSResponseHeaders` entry. Authorization and existing supported key/Anthropic headers are explicit; requested custom headers remain accepted and are deduplicated on preflight. Both original CORS tests fail against wildcard-only headers. Additional Gin-server fixtures retain wildcard origins, no credential flag, and 401 for unauthenticated actual requests. This is a local HTTP integration test, not a real-browser or live-provider test.

No changes to fingerprints, fixed model catalogs, dependencies, management bundle, timeout rules, provider scope, cooldown/disable/scheduling/refresh/migration or server configuration. Claude fingerprint remains **2.1.280**, Codex runtime **0.154.0**, standalone catalog-fetch default **0.155.0**; these are project constants, not claims about latest externally installed clients. Generic compatible routes and existing opt-in plugin contracts remain intact.

### Validation and preserved behavior

The selected changes preserve local account/provider isolation, revoked client-key removal, memory cleanup, branding, five-day refresh lead, credential-version/ABA fences, manual disable, independent cooldowns, refresh retry rules and provider boundaries. Client fingerprints and pinned model catalogs are unchanged: Claude Code 2.1.280, SDK 0.112.1, Node v26.3.0; Codex runtime 0.154.0 / catalog fetcher 0.155.0. No new request timeout or size threshold was added. The HTTP handoff fix only orders removal of the already-existing sniff deadline before publishing the connection.

Five targeted regression tests failed by assertion against the old implementation (three packages; no compilation/setup failure), covering nonstream/stream speed, multi-message done-only text, invalid negative RESP length and deadline handoff. The three cancellation tests passed 1,000 repetitions each under race before and after adaptation; the intermittent upstream failure was not reproduced locally. Source ordering establishes the fixture race window. Initial candidate validation also exposed the separate local sparse-usage path and two stale test assertions for the former management title. The speed implementation was corrected; the panel tests now compare the complete response to the actual embedded management bundle while retaining external-override/safe-mode checks. No management asset was changed. The final malformed-type fixture was corrected before the following complete runs.

Toolchain: **Go 1.27.1, darwin/arm64, CGO_ENABLED=1, GOTOOLCHAIN=auto, GOWORK empty**, dedicated temporary GOCACHE/GOTMPDIR, `GOFLAGS=-mod=readonly`. No live provider request, credential access, remote catalog refresh or server operation was performed.

**U7. Guardian cross-model parent credential affinity — 2f6f2924 / issue6826.** Local keys include provider/session/model, so an eligible parent's other-model binding does not currently control a Guardian selection. No observed deployment incident or upstream risk-control assertion is made. Proposal: only the guardian header plus valid parent reference, same actual provider, newest parent selection, target-model eligibility and existing child-binding precedence; ordinary subagents unchanged. Benefits: parent/reviewer account consistency. Risks: concentrated load, mixed-provider association, entitlement, TTL and alias invalidation. Decide whether Guardian is used and this narrow policy is desired; archive if not used. Test same/cross model, child override, disabled/cooled/unauthorized candidates, mixed pools, expiry without parent TTL extension, removal/alias/race. Roll back the new branch/index only, without clearing credential cooldowns. This is a new specific scenario, not reactivation of archived ID84's broad rebind policy.

## Active decision register — user-authorized triage on 2026-10-20

**October27 update:** the current register is 13 topics after U7 was added on October21; U1 now has historical terminal-failure evidence and is prioritized, while its automatic policy remains unapproved. The October27 review adds K6 compression choices and the U5 forced-tool subcase; the October26 review adds conditional K1/U1/U3 evidence; the October25 review supersedes the earlier U1 evidence/prioritization wording below; IDs and all 84 closures are preserved.

**This section supersedes every older statement below that all 111 (or earlier totals) remain pending.** At the user's explicit request to remove unnecessary accumulated decisions, all 111 IDs were reviewed against published source **9807812bd972485322f02012eabc28ee6fd3add6**, the recorded upstream evidence and current fork scope. The upstream review boundary remains v8.0.35 / ae3ee3be; this is backlog triage, not a new upstream range review or runtime implementation.

**84 entries leave the daily pending queue; 27 original entries remain, consolidated into 12 topics: 6 priorities and 6 uncertain topics.** Closure breakdown: 19 excluded feature expansions, 20 decisions to keep the existing policy, 5 already-covered/unnecessary duplicate proposals, and 40 conditional entries archived until an actual consumer or concrete failure exists. Closed does **not** mean implemented, fixed or permanently impossible. In particular, plugin lifecycle risks remain activation checks, and existing optional Home/plugin/store code is not deleted. No live server/plugin/store usage was assumed from historical records.

For future sync runs: start from this register. Do not restore closed IDs to the daily count merely because the user has not replied, an upstream follow-up landed, or older ledger paragraphs say pending. Reopen only for a materially new supported-path failure, a concrete consumer/enablement change, or explicit user request, and record the new evidence. Do not add unsupported-provider features, generic refactors, or unconsumed SDK/config options as approval requests. A retained topic is not implementation authorization; subcases can be approved separately. Preserve existing approved repairs and their tests.

### Priority topics

**K1. Configuration and auth-file write consistency — original IDs 18, 59, 92, 97, 105.** Prioritize existing native paths: explicit false/deleted nested settings, mutable management configuration, old watcher snapshots and delete/recreate events. New local diagnostics still lose explicit false and retain an obsolete strict-mode=true. Keep file-digest and temporary-retention policies separately decidable; no v8 migration. Decide fail-closed versus retain-current on unreadable/incomplete files. Test real temporary files, save/readback, concurrent setters, stale-read/new-save, deletion, ABA and reload. Roll back coordination code/config backups without restoring rotated tokens.

**K2. Background refresh job identity — original IDs 5.** Queue jobs still carry only auth ID; the worker calls refreshAuth without the enqueue epoch. Completion fencing exists, so this is not a claim that old tokens overwrite new ones. Propose only job ownership/epoch and pending-release bookkeeping. Preserve five-day refresh lead and retry policy. Test queued replacement, disable/re-enable, cancellation/restart, full queues and race. Roll back task bookkeeping only.

**K3. Quota evidence and late-success ordering — original IDs 79, 100, 103.** Keep confirmed quota evidence when cancellation races with it, while pure cancellation must not cool credentials. A new diagnostic reproduces old success clearing newer auth-level cooldown when Model is empty. Fix that narrow ordering rather than replacing every dispatch counter. Item103 remains a reproduction/investigation task: existing 401-recovery controls do not prove every credential-quota/model-capacity shadow scenario. Preserve scope, deadline monotonicity, ABA/manual disable and healthy siblings. Test cancellation/429, older success/new failure, independent cooldowns and restart. Roll back only new classification/watermark logic; never clear existing cooldowns.

**K4. Shutdown and Codex WebSocket lifecycle — original IDs 7, 87.** Shutdown context is created at Run startup; executor replacement and credential removal may close all execution sessions. Prioritize the shutdown timer placement separately from connection preservation. Decide which proxy/auth/routing changes must invalidate old connections. Test long-running service shutdown, active stream draining, shared-store reload and per-auth removal. Roll back lifecycle wiring without introducing inference timeouts.

**K5. Management-secret protection before remote-store bootstrap — original IDs 99.** Conditional but important: code can upload initial Git/Object/Postgres template before LoadConfig hashes the management secret. No real secret or remote store was accessed, and no leak is asserted. Propose v7-only hash-and-verify before upload, including failed bootstrap/restart; complex aliases that retain plaintext should fail closed. Confirm whether those stores are used. Test fake upload bytes, aliases/duplicates, empty/already-hashed secrets and retries. A rollback must disable unsafe initialization rather than restore plaintext upload.

**K6. Request and decompressed-body memory limits — original IDs 88.** Keep as an explicit resource-protection decision, not a claim of a demonstrated attack. Choose a limit from actual image/context requirements and any existing gateway limits; cover chunked and zstd decoded bytes with consistent 413. Default/threshold needs user input. Test small synthetic budgets at boundaries, cancellation and logging readers. Disable only the new limit to roll back.

### Uncertain topics for later discussion

**U1. Rejected-token quarantine and background backoff — original IDs 4, 71.** Need actual repeated 401/invalid_grant symptoms before choosing quarantine/recovery and exponential backoff. Preserve approved five-day lead, version fencing and no ambiguous rotating-token replay. Decide terminal criteria and re-login/manual-refresh recovery. Test stale401/new token, stream401, 429/5xx and disable. Roll back only added state/counters.

**U2. Connection-error classification and short per-minute retry — original IDs 64, 108.** Two separately decidable cases: exact statusless HTTP/2 lifecycle errors and exact Claude per-minute short Retry-After exceptions. Seek sanitized real samples. Do not turn classification into broad network retries or retroactively shorten stored deadlines. Test sent/unsent request boundaries, HTTP status precedence, duplicate/conflicting headers and quota evidence. Roll back narrow classification only.

**U3. Claude cross-request continuity — original IDs 8, 10, 109.** Tool-name continuation, pinned dates and search-result caching are distinct optional stateful features. Need the actual client replay contract. Prefer client replay unless server state is necessary; any cache must isolate caller/auth/session and bound bytes/count/TTL. Benefit is continuity; risks include retained sensitive data, stale dates and cross-session reuse. Test token changes, restarts/eviction, empty-history intent and concurrent branches; roll back only the added caches.

**U4. Pinned model metadata and actual capability matching — original IDs 33, 77, 104.** Retain concrete model/catalog/off-mapping decisions, not a blanket request to copy every new beta. Ask which native models/aliases and compatible routes are used. Pin only selected metadata; decide explicit rejection versus minimal reasoning when off is unsupported. Preserve canonical suffix/body rules and current fingerprints. Test pinned catalog, native/compat/alias, off/auto, count_tokens and minimum betas; roll back metadata/mapping only.

**U5. Concrete tool-protocol compatibility — original IDs 49, 81, 111.** Retain only out-of-int64 schema bounds, complete Codex-to-Claude tool search, and web_search history/declarations. Reject generic lossy schema cleanup. Need real tools/errors or a large-tool consumer; each subcase is separately selectable. Do not auto-enable paid search or silently drop Lite history. Test numeric fidelity, call/result/history, mixed routes, tools null/empty, tool_choice and HTTP/WS/compact. Roll back narrow converters/flags independently.

**U6. Mixed quota/manual-suspension catalog visibility — original IDs 86.** Only the mixed-state catalog presentation remains undecided; single-credential quota visibility and healthy siblings are already covered. Decide whether temporarily unavailable models should remain visible. No scheduler change is proposed. Test mixed/manual/expired/no-timestamp cases and cache invalidation; roll back catalog aggregation only.

### Corrected historical assessment and validation

Item96's earlier statement that registerResolvedModelsForAuth publishes without a final check was incorrect. The actual path calls service.applyAuthModelRegistration -> Manager.ApplyModelRegistration, holding the manager read lock while checking registration epoch, disabled/status, provider, prefix, auth kind and attributes, then invokes registry mutation. Local commit102f79aa already supplies this native protection. Items75/96 therefore leave the active queue; configuration snapshot ownership remains K1, and unadopted plugin publication frameworks are not added. This correction does not claim every possible registry/configuration race is solved.

On unchanged production source, four new temporary diagnostic tests were run with `go test -json -count=1 ./internal/config ./sdk/cliproxy/auth -run ^TestTriage`: one publication-fence test passed (five subcases: prefix, attributes, disabled, replacement, current), and three tests failed as intended by correctness assertions, proving two configuration-save gaps and late auth-level success clearing newer cooldown. These failures support retaining59/100; they are **not** passing fix validation. Temporary fixtures and raw logs are archived outside the repository and excluded from publication.

The source/test/build baseline matches the morning's tested 1642-file manifest **dd3a0c7e3e2f870d26bf4c3915d67bc8abef4b451e0f1cb1ce45739788d0fd50** exactly. Only this ledger is published by this triage; no production or permanent test changes, new full-suite/build claim, deployment, tag, Release, Keeper or skill modification. Focused registration/lifecycle controls were rerun with race instrumentation: 6 top-level tests passed, exit0, no skips (13.51s). The three diagnostic correctness failures were deliberately excluded from that control selection, not fixed or relabeled as passing. Exact documentation commit/remote/CI readback is recorded in the task report. The Chinese report includes every original ID, its closure/reopen condition or retained topic, as well as the detailed decision/testing/rollback plans. Historical reports remain evidence, not the active queue.

### Selected behavior and preservation

- **4337f1f000ffc1bcdfa6f1a05e5b0687842a83aa**: quote/escape access-log paths using `%q`, preventing a decoded newline in a path from injecting a log line. Preserve local request IDs, query/value redaction and log/error policy. Do not import unrelated credits markers.
- **030d17ed947adf8f441c853afb6ddc0637f72fe9**: resolve original request stream intent and tool-name mapping once per conversion state, including the absent-map case. Preserve first-resolution semantics, prepopulated maps, DONE handling, malformed tool arguments, interleaved blocks and the previously published completion/usage repairs. No new cross-request cache.
- **07feedf7b760d33cc14af9cbe517e4ad6aa98aa0 + 4d34502f07f607450857739aff71593835ad07a9**, partially adapted: the EXISTING optional Home `/v1/models` route accepts a valid empty object/empty section list as an empty catalog, but rejects null catalogs/sections, malformed nonempty records and any error envelope, including arrays mixed with models. Keep credential errors as 401 and malformed responses as 502. Preserve local supported-provider filtering, deduplication, token metadata and no fallback to unrelated local models; no Gemini routes or new Home framework. Historical item2 concerns unadopted Home scheduling/ownership changes, not absence of all legacy Home code.

Three production files and three regression files contain this batch; eight top-level tests and one microbenchmark are added. Native management atomic patching in d69962e4 is already addressed by local **102f79aa99d32d3a0fe97563d56863f0749b65ac**, with latest-state clones, per-auth persistence gates and credential/registration fences; the existing lifecycle regression passed. Local hasPluginScheduler already performs external callbacks outside its manager lock. Plugin child-state/host-publication changes remain separately pending. No new cooldown, disable, scheduler, credential lifecycle, migration, thinking, catalog or timeout policy is included. Claude fingerprint **2.1.280**, Codex runtime **0.154.0**, catalog-fetch default **0.155.0** remain current source constants, not claims about the latest installed/public clients.

### Actual validation and exact inputs

First baseline with the logging/conversion fixtures produced **4 failing top-level regressions and 1 passing control**. The later Home baseline produced **3 failing top-level regressions and 1 passing metadata/filtering control**, demonstrating empty-list rejection and array-error masking. The first logging fixture patch failed to apply and a zero-matching-test run followed; the first Home fixture had a struct-literal compile error. Those setup failures/zero-match run are retained as diagnostics, not counted as defect reproductions or acceptance. Corrected fixtures reproduced old behavior before production edits.

### Remaining decisions — 108 historical plus 3 additions

The complete Chinese report retains all **111** individual entries with current behavior, reason, proposed scope, benefit/risk, user question and planned tests/rollback. It distinguishes inherited evidence from today's checks. No pending decision is implemented or approved by this scheduled run; previously approved narrow fixes remain approved.

109. **Claude web-search result cache (9d453bc0, ab9c06b4).** No such result cache exists locally. Upstream adds 24-hour sliding retention and later Home KV, which is not standalone file persistence. Propose explicit opt-in in-memory storage with caller/auth/session/tool isolation and byte/count limits; defer Home/distributed/disk storage. Benefit: replay continuity; risks: retained sensitive data, cross-session disclosure, memory growth and violating explicit history removal. Decide whether needed, TTL/bytes, missing-cache behavior and empty-history intent. Test isolation, token changes, expiry, eviction, multiline/stream/count_tokens and race; roll back only new cache/enablement and clear only its data.
110. **Claude API-key never/uncloak identity (084b9ca7).** Local never preserves native passthrough. Upstream strips CLI/billing/system identity, user_id and selected betas. Propose a separate explicit cleanup mode preserving existing never semantics. Benefit: ordinary API-key identity consistency; risks: native detection, billing markers, user system content and capability betas change. Decide whether to introduce a mode or redefine never, and field allowlist. Test OAuth/API-key, native/generic clients, header overrides, system preservation, messages/stream/count_tokens; roll back wire cleanup only, not fingerprints or credentials.
111. **Codex web_search_call replay/tool reconciliation (e7d4c731).** No local ensureWebSearchToolForHistory. Upstream injects web_search on standard requests and removes search history on Lite. Propose explicit current-tool permission before adding declarations, otherwise a request error; decide Lite filtering independently. Benefit: prevent replay 400s; risks: unrequested paid tools, tool_choice violations and lost context. Decide automatic enabling versus explicit error, and lossless rejection versus Lite history removal. Test native/compat HTTP/WS/compact, absent/null/empty tools, preview declarations, finalizer ordering and non-search controls; roll back only new helper call sites.

Historical evidence updates:

- **2** clarifies existing optional Home catalog/client versus unadopted distributed scheduling ownership. Today's decoder fixes do not enable Home or change dispatch rights.
- **4/71**, feb63984: rejected current access-token state and immediate background refresh on unexpired/stream 401 are not authorized. Keep approved five-day refresh lead rather than upstream 96 hours, version/ABA fences and no ambiguous rotating-token replay. Decide isolation/recovery conditions separately; test stale401/new token/all-model scope/manual disable/race; roll back added rejection/backoff state only.
- **11**, 344e0ef9: shared Claude remote-compact extraction is still dependent on unapproved inference/capsule behavior; exclude native Kimi/Meta. Define preserved content/cost and replay validation before any independent implementation.
- **15/82**, d50a1995/c95c3e7b/ae3ee3be: SIWC v8 binding has five-minute flows, generated client IDs, exclusive file creation/merge, cancellation and legacy-callback/symlink guards. Local SIWC is absent; do not claim a local security repair. Decide opt-in entrypoints, identity and storage independently from v8 migration. Test expiry, duplicate/cancel/edit/delete races and file types; rollback disables SIWC while preserving newly rotated credentials, never restores old tokens.
- **16/63**, d69962e4: native PatchAuth is already equivalent or stronger. Only plugin virtual-source child patching remains a proposal, preserving latest token/version and per-auth persistence gate. Test refresh/edit/ABA/multi-child concurrency and rollback plugin wiring only.
- **23/63**, 1ad175b6: local callback snapshot already releases manager lock and lacks the cross-priority method in the upstream lock cycle. Host still calls manager publication under its mutex, but no local deadlock is claimed. If a real consumer/fixture justifies it, move callbacks outside the lock with a publication fence; test reentrancy/stale publication/unload and rollback ordering only.
- **33/83**, 8df4e42a: fallback gpt-5.5 currently has Lite=false/effort-update=false; some exact templates advertise native capabilities. Propose route-specific capability gates for actual compatible/plugin consumers, with pinned exact-template/alias/mixed-route tests, separately from missing-template fallback. Do not refresh catalogs or synthesize a model.
- **45**, signature commits 7a0b7563/a4660260/cc5682c5/674dbe8f/be9fbd94/a68c7d8e/29076c98: retain strict local GPT format/size checks. New families, GPT/Grok RouterSuffix, alias targets, replay ordering and openrouter.ai suffix policy change trust rules. Require a real compatible consumer and explicit accepted formats/targets; test malformed/oversized/cross-family/native cases and rollback only the compatibility branch. Do not expose removed providers.
- **97/105**, 7484a521: no local IncompleteFiles/pendingProviderReloadAuths. Distinguish valid empty/deleted files, incomplete synthesis and read errors before retaining old auth/indexes. Decide retention interval and whether use continues; pair with the still-pending freshness digest fence. Test interrupted writes/reload/deletion/recreation/multi-ID/disable/race; never resurrect revoked tokens or roll back credential files.
- **107**, 50cfd238: the management UI actually calls model-definitions, whose local endpoint restricts codex/claude. If needed, limit read-only fallback to explicitly enabled plugin providers with native priority; do not expose arbitrary registry providers. Decide UI need separately from credential-bound availability. Test enabled/unknown/disabled plugins, native collisions, static/dynamic fallback and field contracts; rollback endpoint extension only.

Discussion boundary from the invoked skill: “Before changing existing cooldown, disable, scheduling, credential-refresh/lifecycle or migration semantics, discuss a concrete execution plan with the user unless that plan is already authorized in the active task.” Its behavioral-tradeoff rule also covers the new cache, identity and replay decisions. Independent selected fixes proceed under standing publication authorization; unrelated provider-only updates remain excluded rather than becoming new approval requests.

### Selected behavior and local preservation

Partially adapt **4f2e52709dcdb1116c45172d5407c7f626997648 / issue 6707**:

- A compatible Chat Completions backend feeding Claude must provide a completion signal (`[DONE]` or a nonempty finish reason). A clean EOF without either now fails instead of synthesizing successful `message_stop`; buffered usage is retained on the failure. Existing Responses completion recovery and ordinary Chat Completions EOF compatibility remain unchanged.
- Claude forwarding tracks complete JSON SSE events across chunk boundaries, detects clean closure without `message_stop`, separates errors from partial lines/events, and avoids duplicate error payloads. Nested tool data named `message_stop` cannot establish completion. Completed bare-message compatibility and legitimate terminal delimiters remain supported.
- Native and translated Claude execution now assemble multiline JSON data with the existing **bounded** helper in `executor/helps`; diagnostics derive continuity IDs from the same assembled data. Preserve the local explicit upstream error classifier, status/cooldown scope, cancellation, OAuth tool-name restoration and completion-before-disconnect handling. No new timeout, scheduler or credential lifecycle rule is introduced.
- Keep the fork's initial-error policy: an executor rejected by core before first payload still produces the existing HTTP500 `empty_stream` JSON; an empty forwarding channel lacking a terminal event yields HTTP502. A stream whose HTTP status is already committed retains that status with an SSE error. Do not import upstream's unconditional 200 expectation.

Native simple-EOF detection already existed locally and was confirmed by passing baseline controls. Gemini/Vertex/Kimi executors and the deferred apply_patch integration are excluded. No model catalog, fingerprint, thinking, exclusion, plugin identity, cooldown or deployment changes are included. Claude Code fingerprint stays **2.1.280**, Codex runtime **0.154.0**, standalone catalog-fetch default **0.155.0**; these are project constants, not assertions about globally latest client releases.

### Remaining decisions — additions and revised evidence

All previous 103 unresolved items remain pending. The following five additions make **108**, not 108 new updates. No existing authorization for a narrow billing-boundary or credential-result fence is expanded to these new policies. The full Chinese report carries every individual historical item, current evidence, proposed scope, benefit/risk, question, and test/rollback plan.

104. **Model-specific thinking-off mapping (ad9e456d, issue6698).** Current canonical pipeline/metadata do not carry DisableType or the new adaptive-only off fallback. Proposal: separately assess Sonnet5.5 between_tools and Opus/Fable lowest-effort adaptive with pinned metadata. Benefit: avoid model-specific 400s; risk: an explicit off request still reasons/incurs cost and capability/plugin APIs change. Decide whether to map automatically or reject unsupported off, and which models/aliases are allowed. Test none/zero/disabled/auto, suffix precedence, budget/level, display/binding cleanup, native/compat/count_tokens and old/unknown models. Roll back only mapping/catalog changes, not credentials or fingerprints.
105. **Unversioned auth-file snapshot freshness (c4c1d43e, issue6709).** Current credential/ABA fences do not bind file synthesis to disk bytes. Proposal: reproduce a synthetic old-read/new-save/old-publish ordering, then check canonical digest under the per-auth update gate without global-lock I/O. Benefit: stale observations cannot overwrite newer persisted state; risk: unreadable/deleted files, TOCTOU and source-lifecycle differences. Decide native-file-only scope and whether failed reads retain current runtime or mark unavailable. Test ABA, replacement/deletion, JSON reformatting, I/O failure and independent-auth concurrency/race. Roll back the digest gate only; never restore old tokens.
106. **OAuth exclusion priority and write contracts (b015f208/94d1a9a0/1a28c61e/bb428247/3e309fb4/69d0ff8e).** Local behavior unions account and global rules; upstream replaces globals with nonempty account rules and changes reload/config fencing, lenient loads and strict writes. Proposal: keep union default until deciding whether globals are mandatory restrictions or defaults; assess validation separately with actual management consumers. Benefit: flexible account rules and consistent refresh; risk: reopening globally blocked models and rejecting old client input. Decide override policy and array/null versus legacy string acceptance. Test global A/account B, empty/null, legacy aliases, malformed values, config/plugin sources, original IDs versus aliases, concurrent PATCH/reload and race. Back up rules and roll back mappings without clearing auth state; dependencies on items92/96 remain pending.
107. **Credential-bound plugin static catalogs (d7005b63/adc436ff/2422c6b0).** Local catalog-only plugins can publish synthetic clients and append models after native exclusion filtering. Proposal: require routable credentials for advertised availability, filter plugin original IDs, and reserve native models during executor arbitration, independently of item106's priority choice. Benefit: no fictitious availability/exclusion bypass; risk: existing display-only catalogs disappear or plugin/native arbitration changes. Decide actual plugin consumers and whether all exposed models must be callable. Test no-auth/disabled/deleted, two auths with different rules, native collisions, aliases/prefixes and reload/race. Roll back registry strategy only, preserving credentials and configuration.
108. **Per-minute short-retry cooldown exception (db1bab01, issue6719).** Current conservative header/billing-boundary policy remains. Proposal: only three exact positive per-minute messages, one valid Retry-After and no quota-status evidence may ignore an unclassified unified reset; preserve grace, scope and monotonic stored deadlines. Decide whether this precise exception is allowed and whether a real sanitized sample is required; recommend no retroactive shortening of stored cooldowns. Test mixed/negated text, casing/duplicate headers, malformed/expired/long retry, quota windows, fast/standard paths, late429/ABA; roll back parsing entry points without clearing cooldowns.

### Pending decision updates

**100 unresolved items: 97 historical + 3 new.** Keep IDs 1–97 and their dated scopes; silence or another scheduled run is not approval. The Chinese delivery report lists all 100 individually, including current behavior, refs, proposal, benefit/risk, question and tests/rollback. The former 78-item list was already cumulative, not 78 changes from a single day.

**92 — amended: config snapshot ownership and acknowledged writes** (`1631875b`, plus `e235fb3d`, `e230a04b`, remaining `81573f04`). Local SetConfig accepts the shared pointer, scalar setters run outside the persist lock, and watcher hashing does not identify the bytes actually parsed. Include mutation/save atomicity, stale source rejection and serialized watcher application in the staged ownership plan. A setter lock alone does not establish immutable ownership. Define whether a 200 save means durable-only or runtime activation; test sequential PUT readback, a save between read and publication, writes between hash and parse, reload-callback writes, clone independence and race. Roll back snapshot/publication changes without restoring old credentials. This run reviewed code and upstream tests; it did not claim a local reproduction or repair of this issue.

**98. Claude operator header overrides** (`9db0729f`, `4e8a4125`, issue5093). Discuss beta strip/append, diagnostics injection and request-ID modes separately from model catalogs. Recommend default-preserving, explicitly scoped options only for confirmed gateways. Benefits are interoperability/control; risks are losing tool/cache betas, identity changes and tracing correlation. Decide global versus per-credential scope and modes. Test native/reconstructed/helper, OAuth/API-key, messages/stream/count_tokens, custom-header precedence, unknown betas and payload/header coupling. Roll back options to current defaults, then the additive fields.

**99. Remote config seed secret protection** (`f9437700`, issue6628). Local Object/Postgres/Git bootstrap seeds before the normal load hashes the management secret. No actual secrets or remote store were inspected. Upstream also changes alias/layout rewrites, refuses residual plaintext and deletes a just-copied Git template on refusal; v8 migration is not approved. Recommend a prioritized v7-only upload guard with bcrypt, exact safe-byte validation and repeated-start enforcement, excluding v8 normalization. Agree whether unsafe shared aliases/repeated values fail initialization, and whether to retain the original file with mandatory revalidation instead of deleting the copy. Merely moving LoadConfig earlier is insufficient because writeback errors are ignored and alias plaintext can remain. Benefit: no plaintext seed/history; risk: initialization refusal or destructive YAML rewrite. Test fake Object/SQL/Git uploads, hash/no-secret/alias/merge/duplicates/null/long-secret/read-only/write failure and repeat starts; assert no unsafe upload and preserve comments/siblings. Roll back code while preserving safe hashes; do not restore plaintext or delete remote history. Historical-store auditing needs separate authorization.

**100. Model-less stale-success cooldown clearing** (`35af74f9`, issue6643). Existing local model timestamps and active credential quota guards are already equivalent for their covered scopes. A diagnostic confirms an auth-level, empty-Model gap. Recommend narrowing to that clear gate and auditing restore/alias retention; do not replace all dispatch stamps or lifecycle rules implicitly. Decide old unstamped SDK behavior and restart ordering. Benefit: prevent premature reuse; risk: blocking legitimate recovery. Preserve version/ABA/manual disable/count-token contracts; test old/new outcomes, model/auth/credential scope, streams, refresh retries, restore/alias, zero-stamp clients and race. Roll back the independent gate/metadata, retaining stored cooldowns and credentials.

These pending changes follow the skill's discussion boundary for protected policies and substantial new configuration/ownership choices. The independently validated translator fix is publishable without waiting for them.

### Pending decision updates

**97 unresolved items: 91 historical + 6 new.** Keep IDs 1–91 and their prior dated plans. No reply or scheduled run authorizes them. Items 47 (Chat-to-Claude default token caps), 49 (out-of-int64 schema bounds), 64 (HTTP/2 closed-connection cooldown/retry), 77 (Haiku 5.5 catalog), and 81 (tool-search cache anchor) gain the evidence described in the table. The default-cap and schema changes are policy choices, not bundled into ordinary protocol fixes. The delivery report carries every item individually with current behavior, upstream references, proposal, benefits/risks, question and tests/rollback.

**92. Immutable runtime configuration** (`e235fb3d`, `e230a04b`, remaining `81573f04`): local management/runtime still share some mutable configuration and registration still reads `s.cfg`; existing generation locks do not establish immutable ownership. Discuss copy-on-write ownership, per-registration snapshots and management PATCH activation timing before the wider config/startup/watcher refactor. Preserve local generation, disable and credential fences; omit Home/v8. Test concurrent management edits, startup registration, reload/shutdown, deep copies, old/new cache identity and race. Roll back the snapshot layer/callers without changing persisted credentials. The separately selected plugin-host lock is complete and does not wait on this item.

**93. HTTP transport decorator** (`51952a7b`): optional SDK per-attempt observation after transport selection; no confirmed local consumer. Ask which embedder and allow-listed fields need it. If approved, limit to supported HTTP factories, preserve proxy/uTLS/close semantics and wrap once. Test nil identity, context isolation, translated payload/status, cancellation/stream lifecycle and race; roll back additive API and factory hooks. Do not claim WS coverage or import Antigravity pools.

**94. Plugin config defaults** (`50eaed0d`, JSON sanitization fragment of `345cc9d5`): optional Default/clone/management exposure absent locally. Clarify consumer, sensitive-value masking and display-only versus config-fill behavior. Include sanitization with the field; test arbitrary JSON/invalid values, clone independence, secrets and old clients. Revert optional presentation fields only. No existing local vulnerability claim.

**95. TLS handshake timeout** (`345cc9d5`, `b3dc6c3f`): default-off custom-uTLS handshake limit starts after TCP/CONNECT; requires an explicit exception to AGENTS.md and approval of typed retry/no-cooldown classification. Agree scope, default/seconds and root request config semantics. Test silent TLS/CONNECT, caller cancellation, errors.Join, successful long streams, proxy/session reuse, retry rounds/status-bearing errors and race. Disable with zero first; revert independent timeout/classifier changes without connection read timeouts or state resets.

**96. Model publication fencing** (`08e5762a`): local final registry publication lacks the upstream atomic auth-identity plus registry-epoch gate; existing credential-version guards are separate. Discuss stale plugin-result discard/recompute and mutation-lock/side-effect order. Test deterministic old/new prefix, removed/re-registered auth, manual disable/re-enable, execution-only generations, plugin stale update, ABA/late outcomes and race. Roll back final-publication gate only, without clearing state.

**97. Recreated credential file deletion** (`5fc580b1`): whole-file-removal provenance plus fresh strict file synthesis changes whether queued deletion is applied. Decide whether an unreadable/temporarily invalid recreated file retains old auth or stops use; distinguish actual per-ID/config deletes. Test delete/recreate/subset plugin IDs, uncertain reads, relative/case paths, manual disable, queue ordering, ABA and race. Roll back reconciliation code without deleting files or replaying credentials. Coordinate item 89 multi-auth ownership separately.

The skill requires discussion before changing cooldown, disable, scheduling, credential-refresh/lifecycle or migration semantics; other new architectural/configuration extensions require their concrete scope choices. This run does not infer authorization from the user's unrelated isolation feature branch. Deployment history below is unchanged; no server was contacted.

### Selected scope and local preservation

- **Claude cache-control presence (`be8c35f2`)**: treat null and other nonobjects as absent when counting/enforcing cache breakpoints, ordering TTLs and deciding automatic tool/system/message injection. This fixes real marks being removed, one-hour TTL being downgraded and injection being suppressed by nulls. Deliberately do **not** import upstream's stricter `type == ephemeral` acceptance: empty and custom objects remain accepted. Retain thread-reserved budget, last tool/system priority, deferred-tool skipping, native message placement, blank-system behavior and approved billing/header behavior.
- **Chat-to-Codex call IDs (`b0debb35`)**: shorten only over-64-byte IDs using a stable SHA-256 suffix, reserve collisions with legal IDs, and pair outputs via the original source ID. The local adaptation additionally keeps the prefix on a UTF-8 boundary. Preserve legal IDs, missing-ID generation, ambiguous duplicates, custom/function tools and native Responses IDs. No new dependency, fingerprint/catalog/prompt upgrade, provider route, cooldown/disable/scheduling/refresh change or configuration is included.

The two production files and two regression files are the complete code selection. Six new top-level tests cover invalid cache values, object compatibility, cache limit/order/TTL/injection, long shared-prefix IDs, collision with a legal ID, custom tools, both stream flags and Unicode boundaries. Against the old implementation, four top-level tests failed assertions and two preservation controls passed (exit 1, 7.74 s); there was no setup/compile failure. They now pass in the final full and race suites.

### Pending decisions and protected scope

**91 items: 80 historical, 11 new.** Stable numbers 1–80 and their dated evidence remain effective; the delivery report lists every item individually with sources, current state, reason, proposal, benefits/risks, question and tests/rollback. The earlier 78-item list already included historical work; items 79–80 were added October 11. Repeated schedules and no reply do not authorize any undecided scope. Item 79 is now released, so release availability no longer blocks it; approval of its narrow cooldown/version/notification plan is still required. The two ordinary release-pending candidates are now the selected repairs above and are not counted as discussion items.

**81.** Claude client tool search: gate default-off request/response/history translation and per-credential model advertisements together. Preserve local checked translation, tool pairing and caching; omit Home. Discuss actual Codex-to-Claude need and trusted custom backends before adding this large protocol/configuration family. Test collisions, deferred references, streaming, mixed routes, disabled goldens and request-pinned reload behavior; rollback the independent family or disable the gate after checking existing search histories.

**82.** Sign in with ChatGPT: new login/binding, dual credential partitions, locked persistence, endpoint/body/header changes and refresh scheduling. No local SIWC path exists. Discuss explicit entrypoints, binding identity and migration before implementation; preserve five-day outer refresh and local ABA/credential-version/retry guards. Use mocked OAuth, concurrent partition rotation, failed/late writes and all stores; rollback the optional path while preserving credential backups, never replay old rotating tokens.

**83.** Missing default model template: current pinned catalog contains gpt-5.5. Upstream falls back to the first template and its fetch tool can synthesize a visible gpt-5.5 entry from another model. Discuss explicit error versus a chosen compatible fallback; do not synthesize models or replace catalogs/prompts implicitly. Test missing/empty/incompatible templates and alias capabilities; rollback fixed template policy only.

**84.** Session-affinity rebind policy: new any/quota option affects selection, result handling, priorities and LCP state. Discuss whether transient failover should retain the original binding, which failures rebind, and whether scope is explicit session IDs only. Test manager-level failure classes, quota/auth/expiry/manual disable, healthy siblings, stale versions and race. Roll back the option/independent selector change without clearing cooldowns; omit unadopted LCP frameworks.

**85.** v8 plugin quota contract: quota_plugin_id selection, fused-plugin filtering, SDK exposure and hiding unsupported v8 quota_probe depend on the unadopted v8/plugin quota framework. Keep historical items 15 and 25 separate. Ask for an actual consumer before adding the smallest compatible API. Test matching precedence, fusion during DescribeQuota, cancellation and v0/v8 round trips; rollback optional fields without changing cooldowns.

**86.** Mixed manual/quota discovery accounting: local 581aa6b9 and 2067d9f3 already handle credential_quota, single-credential visibility, absolute expiry and healthy siblings. The upstream diagnostic passes those parts but fails two assertions for one credential_quota-only client without a quota timestamp plus one manual-suspended client. This is a catalog-policy disagreement, not an end-to-end scheduling proof. Discuss hiding versus keeping such mixed pools visible; if approved, adapt only both catalog counting paths, preserving local deadlines, cache generations and manual disable. Test manager projections, overlap/expiry/provider combinations and race; rollback catalog counting only.

**87.** Codex WebSocket reload/removal: local executor replacement and removal fallback can close all sessions. Upstream shares session stores across replacement and pins connection configuration; removal can target one auth. Discuss which ordinary reloads may preserve connections and which proxy/auth/disable changes must invalidate them. Test two credentials/stores, reload, target changes, deletion, required replay and race. Roll back the independent lifecycle change with safe reconnect; exclude xAI.

**88.** Request-body limit: default-off configurable byte limit spans middleware, compressed/chunked reads, logging, plugin auth, management and 413 mapping. Discuss required limit and endpoint scope before adding configuration; do not import v8 or removed providers. Test length/chunked/zstd/overflow/auth-before-body paths and unbuffered SSE; disable or revert only the limiter.

**89.** Plugin auth.save identity and multi-auth lifecycle: local save builds one record; upstream parses multiple child records, reuses IDs, preserves state and removes stale same-file auth/model registrations. Discuss actual plugin usage and whether one physical file can own multiple identities; prefer a narrow ID correction if sufficient. Coordinate historical items 16–19. Test watcher/save interleavings, custom IDs, disabled children, stale registration cleanup and race; rollback mapping changes without deleting credential files.

**90.** Pre-first-token SSE keepalive: default-off option commits headers during bootstrap and therefore converts later bootstrap HTTP failures into SSE errors. Discuss actual slow-prefill need and accepting this error contract. Keep local private-event/error precedence and terminal handling. Test deterministic tick boundaries, cancellation/writes, stalled executors, headers and race; disable the option to recover original HTTP status behavior, without introducing post-connection upstream timeouts.

**91.** Shared store fragments in Antigravity persistence commit: exclude its provider-specific probes and new metadata preemption/save-gate framework. Local Git metadata Save already continues when local content matches; Object/Postgres still short-circuit. Actual deployed backend and remote-write failure are not established. Discuss a narrow remote consistency retry fix only for used stores, and assess Git context cancellation separately from historical item 68. Test matching local content after failed remote save, cancellation, concurrent refresh/disable/version fences and load/save ordering; rollback Save-only changes without migration or token reset.

The current [skill](</Users/vpromisex/.codex/skills/codexproxy-upstream-sync/SKILL.md>) states: **“Before changing existing cooldown, disable, scheduling, credential-refresh/lifecycle or migration semantics, discuss a concrete execution plan with the user unless that plan is already authorized in the active task.”** This is the reason the undecided protected scopes above remain pending. Larger protocol/configuration and consumer-dependent additions also require the concrete choices described above. Independent selected repairs are authorized for normal commit/push without another confirmation.

Publication is recorded separately after exact remote verification. Fingerprints remain Claude Code **2.1.280**, Codex runtime **0.154.0**, catalog-fetch default **0.155.0**. No tag, Release, server operation/deployment, CI configuration change, CodexKeeper edit or skill edit is included; the October 11 deployment record remains historical and unchanged.

### New decisions and historical carry-forward

There are now **80 unresolved decision items: 78 historical items plus 2 new items**. The October 10 report's 78 already included **76 older items and 2 October 10 additions** (TTFT and Home availability). They are not 78 newly discovered changes. All 78 are carried forward individually in the delivery report with their existing applicability, proposal, benefit/risk, question and test/rollback plan. The two ordinary main-only repair candidates above await release and are counted separately. Permanent exclusions are also separate. A scheduled invocation, silence, or generic sync authorization does not approve protected decisions.

**79. Cancellation after an actual quota failure (`5dbd92a0`).** Current manager/executor cancellation ordering can discard known quota state, allowing another selection of an exhausted credential. Proposal: preserve and record only a confirmed quota 429 with valid reset/Retry-After evidence even when the client context is cancelled, and deliver the upstream error before disconnect notification. Preserve model-versus-credential scope, monotonic deadlines, manual disable, healthy siblings, attempt timestamps and all local state/version fences; pure cancellation, EOF, disconnect or absent retry evidence must not invent cooldown. Benefit: avoid repeated exhausted-account attempts. Risks: stale errors cooling changed credentials, widened cooldown scope, or blocking/premature WebSocket cleanup. **Decision needed:** approve this bounded recording/ordering repair, and specify whether to wait for a containing release (the default) or explicitly include this unreleased commit. Tests must include the three failing manager cases, pure cancellation, invalid/missing retry evidence, model/shared quota, stale/late/ABA results, refresh retry, unbuffered bootstrap and native mock-WebSocket error/failed frames with repeated new connections; run relevant race suites. Roll back only the isolated recording/notification branch and tests, without clearing persisted cooldown state or undoing existing fences.

**80. Usage authentication-source metadata (`37fe2608`).** Current usage consumers receive the existing API-key field but no provider/native classification. Proposal only when an actual consumer requires it: add optional attribution/context fields with explicit unknown versus false semantics and preserve existing key exposure/access boundaries; retain backwards-compatible queue/plugin serialization and existing inference/auth routing. Benefit: usage attribution across native and external auth providers. Risks: schema/ABI incompatibility, misclassifying missing metadata and exposing extra principal details. **Decision needed:** is there a concrete usage consumer, and which minimal fields/transport does it require? Recommend deferring absent that need. Validate native/plugin/unauthenticated and plain/Gin contexts, explicit-false precedence, old JSON consumers, queue round trips and concurrency; roll back the optional metadata propagation while keeping old consumer schema accepted. No companion project or production configuration change is included.

The current skill at `/Users/vpromisex/.codex/skills/codexproxy-upstream-sync/SKILL.md` states: **“Before changing existing cooldown, disable, scheduling, credential-refresh/lifecycle or migration semantics, discuss a concrete execution plan with the user unless that plan is already authorized in the active task.”** This blocks decision 79 and other still-undecided protected proposals from implementation, not this independent documentation publication. Decision 80 remains a scoped statistics/schema proposal under the skill's prohibition on importing adjacent statistics/plugin frameworks without need. No new decision is inferred as approved.

## Authorized Claude billing-boundary repair on 2026-10-09

After the detailed scope discussion, the user explicitly approved filtering a unified reset when the overage representative claim is present and both parsed reset times match. This is a narrow adaptation of [80809679](https://github.com/router-for-me/CLIProxyAPI/commit/80809679914f376605e2ae6aa2c5d68f140f98d3), within the already reviewed **v8.0.22** boundary. This follow-up makes no newer upstream freshness claim and does not authorize publication or deployment.

The isolated candidate started from **`dafabedeb2b64e105a42a8a6d101b63dda9deaff`** plus all prior October 9 working changes. Its initial **1619-file** source/test/build manifest exactly matched **`2d0f849c95ebd3ef7e1823edf779d9ce40d20b8b9e0cbae52818a03345fead0f`**. The original 12 unstaged/untracked paths and empty main index were preserved. Only the Claude reset parser and two new regression files were added to that selected source.

### Included behavior and preserved policies

The parser normalizes the representative claim, requires it to contain `overage`, parses both `Anthropic-Ratelimit-Unified-Reset` and `Anthropic-Ratelimit-Unified-Overage-Reset`, and excludes only the unified candidate when the two instants are equal. Independent `Retry-After`/`Retry-After-Ms` and explicit rejected shared-window deadlines retain their existing precedence and longest-deadline selection. Without another valid candidate, existing generic quota backoff applies.

No global seven-day-plus-one-hour filter was imported. Missing shared-window health evidence remains conservative and credential scoped; no account/model reclassification, manual-disable change, cooldown-state clearing or scheduling change is included. A valid explicit long `Retry-After` remains effective even when the unified billing candidate is filtered. Existing installed cooldown deadlines are not rewritten by the parser.

The parser regression covers **16 cases**, including equivalent Unix/RFC3339 timezone representations, normalized claims, different/missing/malformed timestamps or claims, insufficient overage-status-only evidence, short and 8/9-day explicit retries, millisecond precedence and independent shared-window resets. The executor/manager regression covers **8 cases** across streaming and non-streaming execution: generic backoff, short retry, a nine-day explicit retry and shared rejection. It checks actual manager deadlines, retained credential scope and sibling-model exclusion while applicable. Fixtures use local HTTP servers and synthetic credentials.

The deployed Linux CGO-disabled loader cannot load standard dynamic-library plugins. dmit supports that capability but does not currently enable or load any. Native Codex/Claude execution does not require a dynamic plugin. This inventory did not restart services, modify servers, access provider credentials or invoke inference; deployment remains the October 8 source recorded below.

### Scope decisions after user follow-up

The initial synchronization left both proposals unimplemented while the required discussion was pending. The user subsequently approved the narrow Claude billing-boundary filter and asked whether plugins were currently used. The approved filter is implemented and validated in the follow-up above. The plugin proposal remains unimplemented after read-only inventory established no current dynamic-plugin use.

- **Claude billing boundary, approved and integrated:** ignore only a unified reset positively identified as the overage billing boundary by matching parsed unified/overage reset times and the overage representative claim. Retain explicit long `Retry-After`, shared-window deadlines, monotonic state and conservative handling of missing health evidence. Do not import the blanket seven-day cap or the absence-as-healthy classification. Rollback is limited to this parser/test change; the parser does not clear already-installed production cooldowns.
- **Plugin disable cleanup, deferred:** a future authorized change would route global disable and deselection through existing detach/shutdown cleanup under the apply lock, preserving selected siblings, callback cleanup, cancellation and re-enable behavior. Tests must follow the local lifecycle implementation; upstream's separate HTTP-operation registry is absent locally. No plugin configuration or lifecycle change is needed for the current native Codex/Claude deployment. Revisit when plugins are enabled or the user selects this lifecycle repair explicitly.

All older deferred families remain separate, including explicit prompt caching, thread-alias storage, refresh queue/rejection policies, payload finalization, catalogs, Home and v8 migration.

| **Credential-version fencing** — [6920ed5d](https://github.com/router-for-me/CLIProxyAPI/commit/6920ed5dfc3556588df553a44878254e8d9577d4), [935aa6e3](https://github.com/router-for-me/CLIProxyAPI/commit/935aa6e3eac58f6d422242a44ac0ecb7de211809) | Added manager-owned `Auth.CredentialVersion`, distinct from registration, availability and general generation. Registration/reload initializes it; changes to provider/access/refresh/ID-token/API-key material advance it, including successful refresh. Ordinary configuration and result bookkeeping do not. Execution results carry the selected version; stale success/401/429 results cannot change current counters, availability or affinity. Refresh and prepared-metadata merges, including the refresh failure path, reject an obsolete version even after A→B→A edits. Existing manual-disable and independent cooldown protections remain. |
| **Claude missing-thread classification and replay marker** — [5c959cb0](https://github.com/router-for-me/CLIProxyAPI/commit/5c959cb032efb6f2067341915c9fb137532b8d5b) | Recognizes structured `404 + not_found_error` with the verified `thread state` / `previous_message_id` message or `thread_not_found` marker, including wrapped response bodies. Manager request-fault handling returns it without credential rotation or cooldown. Claude JSON, direct and terminal SSE errors preserve upstream fields while adding `error.details.error_code=thread_not_found`; SSE JSON is compacted into one data line. Unrelated model 404s, arbitrary plain text, authentication, quota and server errors keep their existing classification. Client history replay is enabled by the marker; no automatic inference retry or history store is introduced. |
| **Emptied attachment-turn refusal and error propagation** — scoped adaptation of [3b69068e](https://github.com/router-for-me/CLIProxyAPI/commit/3b69068e6c8c028c56b7d0d0467a61d8c66e103c), [f51624aa](https://github.com/router-for-me/CLIProxyAPI/commit/f51624aa35c1c5fdf21e4307ddb3140af3e86525) | Tracks actual lost attachments per source user message in Chat→Claude, Responses→Claude, Claude→Chat, Claude→Codex, Chat→Codex and Responses→Chat conversion. A turn with no representable content, including only blank text beside a dropped attachment, returns request-scoped 400 before upstream dispatch. System/developer text and other turns cannot hide the loss. Representable mixed text/media and native-protocol attachment fields retain their existing behavior. Added checked registry/converter APIs while retaining public byte-returning signatures; wired errors through pipeline, ordinary/compatibility/multi-agent helpers, HTTP/stream/WebSocket/count_tokens, enabled plugin executor preparation and the Responses-shaped Chat handler. The breaking public signature migration in [0f96f568](https://github.com/router-for-me/CLIProxyAPI/commit/0f96f568e4dbf6f84ad7399a74b78344c5eac7e6) remains deferred. |
| **Ambiguous Claude refresh retries** — retry portion of [c163bae4](https://github.com/router-for-me/CLIProxyAPI/commit/c163bae4bcc12df5aa76a1a58ba3f26860be9393) | Only explicit retryable HTTP failures permit another attempt. Transport/reset, response-body read and decode failures stop the immediate retry loop because the refresh token may already have been consumed. Existing 429 blocking, terminal rejection, explicit server-error backoff and cancellation behavior remain. Failure messages now report actual attempts. This does not recover a lost rotated token, disable a credential, add rejected-token selection state or change background refresh scheduling. |

**Compatibility choices.** Credential versions are runtime-only (`json:"-"`). Legacy SDK results or merge bases with version zero retain the earlier compatibility behavior; manager execution and refresh paths always bind a version. Old-version results still reach observation hooks/error events with their original snapshot but do not add statistics to the replacement credential. Reload starts a new registration, so resetting its version to one does not admit old results.

Only the **working payload's** checked error controls dispatch; a bad baseline does not reject a valid repaired working input. Paired buffers remain independent, and stateful plugins retain two invocations. Plugin fallback normalization still precedes its translator and can repair source input. For built-in conversions, target normalization retains its existing position after translation; a proven source-turn loss remains an error even if that hook changes the output. A target-only hook has no reliable source-turn provenance with which to establish recovery, so arbitrary added text cannot clear the error. This makes the earlier plugin-repair recommendation precise without introducing a second validation framework. Legacy plugin translators keep their existing byte-returning contract.

Native Responses `compact` requests retain their attachment fields; there is no newly registered Claude→Responses compact conversion. Unsupported providers, remote file retrieval, alias continuity storage, explicit cache options, terminal/rejected-token policies, refresh queue ownership, global GitHub configuration and other frameworks stay deferred. The earlier attachment-preservation mappings and client fingerprints remain intact. These are four separately scoped repairs, not whole-commit or ancestry merges.

## Deferred-family reassessment on 2026-10-08 — analysis only

**Historical analysis checkpoint:** the subsequent user-authorized implementation above supersedes only the four priority repair dispositions. Other deferred proposals and this diagnostic evidence retain their original scope.

The user requested a careful reassessment and report of previously deferred synchronization. This review compares the **current working implementation**, including the validated October 7–8 changes, against the deferred upstream families through the already reviewed **v8.0.20 / `0f96f568e4dbf6f84ad7399a74b78344c5eac7e6`** boundary. It does not advance the upstream checkpoint or assert that no later upstream commits exist. Official issue reports and the fetched commit objects were checked alongside local call paths. The recommendations below amend the earlier rationale and priority; **none is implemented by this reassessment**.

Main remained at **`89468af557f0a117302785936822e29caa5be652`**, with the same **1604-file** source/test/build manifest **`dc9149d5bda3336e25fd3d2ebe5ebbfd513c8eac49579bb8785abde04133c301`**. Initial ledger SHA-256 was **`702880848bc42ae4ce22918026a94fd5a377470a5505e772bee6b723b1544f4d`**. Only that worktree received temporary diagnostic tests, using fake credentials and mocked transports.
