### Revised recommendations and evidence

| Deferred family | Reassessment |
| --- | --- |
| Attachment refusal and translator error propagation: `3b69068e`, `f51624aa`, `0f96f568` | **Recommend a scoped correctness repair; keep the public SDK signature migration separate.** The October 8 mappings preserve representable media, but do not prevent an unrepresentable attachment-only user turn from disappearing. A mocked Claude executor received `messages=[]` for both an OpenAI opaque file-ID-only turn and an input-audio-only turn. The latter is not a supported Claude input. The proxy should return a request-scoped 400 for an emptied user turn before contacting an upstream. This is a remaining defect, not an equivalent already-synced change. |
| Claude missing-thread 404: [5c959cb0](https://github.com/router-for-me/CLIProxyAPI/commit/5c959cb032efb6f2067341915c9fb137532b8d5b), follow-up `e7b22260` | **Recommend independently of the alias cache.** The bare upstream JSON described in [#6344](https://github.com/router-for-me/CLIProxyAPI/issues/6344) is not recognized by local `IsRequestFault`; a manager diagnostic applies a **12-hour model cooldown** under normal cooling settings. Claude error formatting also lacks `error.details.error_code=thread_not_found`. These gaps exist even without a local cross-request store. The later synthetic/plain-text error repair in [#6443](https://github.com/router-for-me/CLIProxyAPI/issues/6443) remains conditional on adding that producer. Earlier grouping of the entire family behind the store was too broad. |
| Credential versions: [6920ed5d](https://github.com/router-for-me/CLIProxyAPI/commit/6920ed5dfc3556588df553a44878254e8d9577d4), [935aa6e3](https://github.com/router-for-me/CLIProxyAPI/commit/935aa6e3eac58f6d422242a44ac0ecb7de211809) | **Recommend as a high-priority lifecycle repair.** Registration fencing protects delete/re-register; it does not fence token edits within one registration. An old-token 401 bound before `PatchAuth` still cools the new token's model afterward. Separately, an A-to-B-to-A edit lets an old refresh result pass the current value-comparison guard. The ordinary concurrent refresh-versus-different-token protection already works and must be retained. Thus the missing scope is broader than only rare ABA edits for execution results, while the refresh-merge defect is narrower. See [#6416](https://github.com/router-for-me/CLIProxyAPI/issues/6416) and [#6417](https://github.com/router-for-me/CLIProxyAPI/issues/6417). |
| Refresh retry, terminal rejection and queue ownership: `c163bae4`, `50d5fe18`, `81756a57` | **Split into independently reviewable changes.** The local retry classifier returns true for `io.ErrUnexpectedEOF` and an ambiguous connection-reset error. Stopping automatic replay of the same Claude refresh token in those cases is a narrow first repair; [#6288](https://github.com/router-for-me/CLIProxyAPI/issues/6288) reports the rotation hazard. Rejected-token selection/terminal `401 + invalid_grant` behavior and background job ownership need the separate lifecycle contract below. Static inspection confirms queued jobs carry only an auth ID, unlike request-triggered refresh's registration-epoch check. This review does not claim a reproduced queue race or a live provider token-loss incident. |
| Thread tool-alias state: `678a9257`, `a2976eb8`, `e7b22260` | **A real conditional compatibility gap; retain separate implementation scope.** A create request can alias `Read`; a continuation containing only tool results has no reverse map. Both local response restorers return the generated alias unchanged when the map is empty. They do not raise the existing unresolved-alias error in this case. This agrees with the failure mechanism in [#6346](https://github.com/router-for-me/CLIProxyAPI/issues/6346). The gap concerns cloaked requests that actually alias tools and use declaration-free thread continuation; recognized native passthrough does not run that alias pass. |
| Explicit prompt caching: [eb6a768d](https://github.com/router-for-me/CLIProxyAPI/commit/eb6a768d103da08c039c20dfad28aad2c936fbbc) | **Useful opt-in feature; can be adapted without the global payload-finalizer framework.** A Claude-format, cloaked API-key mock with `prompt_cache_options.mode="explicit"` and no client markers still forwards the option and receives **two automatic cache markers**. This confirms the option is not implemented locally, not that a production caller has used it or incurred a measured charge. [#6380](https://github.com/router-for-me/CLIProxyAPI/issues/6380) provides a concrete caller-control use case. Preserve current implicit/default behavior and omit the upstream commit's unrelated advisor-cache default change. |
| Global GitHub token: [7c41cd05](https://github.com/router-for-me/CLIProxyAPI/commit/7c41cd05e433a81b9c702f39e3386c483c1bc69f) | **Continue deferring as configuration convenience.** The local UI is embedded, but it is inaccurate to infer that the server never reads GitHub: the latest-version management endpoint already uses `ResolveGitHubToken` and existing environment precedence. Plugin stores already support explicitly configured `github-token`/`token-env` authentication. The new global YAML source, fallback and hot reload can wait for a concrete private-release/plugin consumer. This runtime setting does **not** configure repository SSH/HTTPS push or resolve a GitHub publication permission problem. |
| Broader payload/catalog/storage/plugin families | **Continue deferring absent a supported consumer or failing fixture.** Payload finalization (`868e137f`), configurable catalogs (`1672be5d` and follow-ups), Store.Save mutation reflection/load gating (`8a01bc02`), reporting/delivery settlement, apply-patch/local-shell bridges, Home/plugin extensions and plugin hook coalescing are not prerequisites for the narrow repairs above. Their separate remaining scope is retained below. No unsupported native provider or third-party encrypted-content behavior is promoted. |

### Concrete adaptation boundaries

**Attachment errors.** Local `sdk/translator/types.go` and `registry.go` still expose byte-only transforms, but `Pipeline.TranslateRequest` already returns `(RequestEnvelope, error)`. An additive checked registration/translation path with compatibility wrappers is a feasible alternative to changing all public converter signatures at once. All internal dispatch paths that can lose a user turn must use the checked path: ordinary and compatibility conversion, Codex multi-agent normalization, HTTP/stream/WebSocket/count_tokens, and enabled plugin executors. The pre-conversion of Responses-shaped input in `/v1/chat/completions` must propagate errors too; even upstream v8.0.20 still discards the converter error at that handler call site (`rawJSON, _ = ...`). A whole-commit import would not prove coverage.

Track content survival **per user turn**, not over the whole conversation. Preserve adjacent representable text, valid file/audio mappings, tool results and system/developer handling. The recommended minimal policy rejects a turn emptied by unsupported content; rejecting every mixed text-plus-unsupported-attachment request would be a further policy expansion. Propagate the **working request's** failure, allowing a repaired working request when only the baseline fails. Preserve independent buffers, native summary/reasoning normalization, existing plugin normalization order and **two hook invocations** for paired inputs with stateful plugins. Decide validation placement from the effective translated payload so a plugin repair is not rejected prematurely. Acceptance tests must assert zero upstream calls on refusal and continued success for representable/mixed/repaired inputs. Rollback is limited to the checked path and its callers, leaving the already validated October 8 mappings intact.

**Missing-thread recovery.** Recognize a verified Claude missing-thread 404, using the structured error type/message or marker and the continuation context where available. Do not classify every 404, arbitrary text containing “thread”, authentication failure, 429 or server failure as a request fault. Preserve unrelated error fields when adding the replay marker. The proposed behavior is to return the same request error without rotating or cooling credentials and let the client replay the full conversation; the proxy does not synthesize history or silently retry inference. Required coverage includes two-credential selection, non-stream/stream errors, wrapped bodies and unrelated model-not-found 404s. This change and its rollback can stand alone; the earlier proposal to roll classification and alias storage back together is superseded.

**Credential versions and refresh.** Add a manager-owned monotonic version for changes to credential material, distinct from registration epoch, disable/enable availability epoch and the general generation that also changes with bookkeeping. Carry it with execution snapshots/results and refresh bases; obsolete outcomes must not change the current credential's availability. Define statistics treatment and legacy SDK results with no version explicitly. Include token edits, successful refresh, reload and change-and-revert, while ordinary configuration/usage changes must not masquerade as new tokens. A value-only check can block a simple late 401 but cannot close the demonstrated ABA gap. Preserve current manual-disable state and independent quota/capacity cooldowns; targeted existing controls for these cases still pass.

The ambiguous-refresh retry decision can be adapted independently: preserve the established explicit HTTP error/backoff classification, stop retrying transport/decode outcomes whose token-consumption result is unknown, and retain cancellation behavior. This does not recover a new token whose response was already lost. Test call counts for malformed success responses, transport failures, cancellation, explicit retryable responses and terminal refresh rejection.

Then assess rejected-token selection (`50d5fe18`, terminal portions of `c163bae4`) against the version of the token that actually failed: an old 401 must not blacklist a replacement token. Distinguish missing expiry, temporary refresh failures and structured terminal rejection; specify manual refresh/login recovery and late success handling. The local independent-cooldown preservation already covers part of [#6415](https://github.com/router-for-me/CLIProxyAPI/issues/6415), but not its new rejected-token state. Queue ownership (`81756a57`) should separately carry registration epoch and a job-owned pending identity through enqueue/start/finish, conditionally release abandoned jobs, and preserve local disabled checks, lead times, per-ID refresh locks and backoff. Test saturated queues, cancellation/restart, delete/recreate and concurrent edits before adoption. No credential file-format or provider migration is needed for these proposals, and they must remain separate rollback units.

**Alias state and explicit caching.** Adopt alias continuity only with an explicit contract for the affected cloaked/thread path. The inspected upstream store is bounded to **1024 message keys**, insertion-ordered, without TTL; keys contain message IDs without caller/auth namespacing. Lookup does not make it LRU. It requires state for every declaration-free continuation on its call path, which is broader than proving the prior turn actually aliased tools. A local adaptation should preserve caller-owned alias identity, choose caller/session/upstream scope deliberately, handle branch/concurrent tool changes, and define bounded eviction, restart and multi-instance replay behavior. Do not turn native passthrough or genuinely tool-free threads into new cache dependencies. Shared storage is not automatically required; a client replay contract or established routing affinity may suffice. Missing local state must produce a recognizable replay error rather than an unresolved generated name. Preserve the existing strict guards when a nonempty map cannot resolve an alias.

Explicit caching can use a request-local policy carried through the existing translation/cloaking/override stages. Strip the proxy option before the wire request and count_tokens; preserve valid client markers/TTLs when explicit, subject to the four-breakpoint/thread reservation limits, and skip automatic injection. Keep OAuth/API-key system-placement differences, strict/advisor/legacy handling, helper TTL rules, signing after final body changes and current behavior when the option is absent. Raw overrides and option precedence require tests; they do not require importing the upstream global finalizer. Date pinning (`6d57ac90`) remains a different tradeoff: stable cache prefixes versus the configured timezone's current date for long-lived sessions and across restarts.

**Remaining framework scope.** The local `persistAuthLocked` already serializes per-ID writes, clones the saved auth and releases the manager mutex before Store.Save; importing store-induced field deltas requires a new ownership contract, not a duplicate lock fix. Payload finalization must first demonstrate a current ordering failure and preserve raw/default/filter, thinking and signing precedence. Catalog configuration must preserve the fork's `--local-model` offline contract rather than inheriting upstream explicit-URL behavior. Keep stateful plugin hook invocation semantics; evaluate reporting, delivery acknowledgments, apply-patch/local-shell capability advertisement and Home/plugin APIs only with their actual supported consumer. Unknown third-party encrypted content (`d2597a24`, [#6399](https://github.com/router-for-me/CLIProxyAPI/issues/6399)) does not justify weakening native signature checks; third-party services belong under openai-compatibility or an enabled plugin. Global GitHub configuration, if later needed, must preserve explicit plugin auth selection, host/redirect scope, reload and cache identity behavior.

### Diagnostic validation and limits

The temporary diagnostics assert **current behavior**, including gaps. Passing them establishes the observations, **not remediation**. The eight top-level diagnostics were:

| Diagnostic | Observed result |
| --- | --- |
| `TestDeferredAuditThread404Cooldown` | Verified missing-thread JSON is not request-scoped; the manager records a 12-hour model cooldown. |
| `TestDeferredAuditMissingThreadMarker` | Claude error rendering omits the client replay marker. |
| `TestDeferredAuditLate401AfterCredentialEdit` | The replacement token survives, but the old request's bound 401 applies cooldown to its model. |
| `TestDeferredAuditRefreshABA` | A-to-B-to-A edits permit the stale refresh result through the current merge guard. |
| `TestDeferredAuditAttachmentRefusalGap` | Opaque file-ID-only and audio-only user inputs reach a mocked Claude upstream as `messages=[]`. |
| `TestDeferredAuditThreadAliasContinuation` | The initial declared tool restores correctly; the declaration-free continuation passes the generated alias unchanged in both non-stream and stream restoration. |
| `TestDeferredAuditAmbiguousRefreshRetry` | Transport/reset and response-decoding examples are classified retryable. This is a classifier test, not live token reuse. |
| `TestDeferredAuditExplicitCacheGap` | A cloaked Claude-format API-key request forwards the unhandled explicit option and receives two auto cache markers. |

An initial probe run had **7 passes / 1 assertion failure** because the alias diagnostic expected an unresolved-alias error. Code inspection and a corrected diagnostic showed the actual empty-map branch passes aliases unchanged. No production code was changed to make this diagnostic pass. Initial diagnostic manifest: **`1e34ffbd8640d04885e1aa29ef4c01a02c2a495b9ec9a72e3a1c93514c911cce`** / 1608 files.

Final diagnostic source was the unchanged main source plus four temporary `deferred_audit_test.go` files under `sdk/cliproxy/auth`, `internal/runtime/executor`, `internal/auth/claude` and `sdk/api/handlers/claude`: **1608 files**, manifest **`eb8228731687a06e87b1b857f002fc8c456acd67e9e4251e420128d107edc540`**. Go **1.27.1**, **darwin/arm64**, dedicated temporary GOCACHE/GOTMPDIR and `GOFLAGS=-mod=readonly`. Both final commands exited **0**, with before/after manifests identical and **no skips**:

- `go test -count=1 -json -run '^TestDeferredAudit' ./sdk/cliproxy/auth ./internal/runtime/executor ./internal/auth/claude ./sdk/api/handlers/claude` — **4 packages / 8 top-level diagnostic tests**, 2.21s.
- `go test -race -count=1 -json -run '^Test(DeferredAudit|Lifecycle_(InflightResultCannotAffectReplacement|InflightFailureCannotUndoDisableEnableReset|RefreshConcurrentChanges|CredentialRecoveryPreservesIndependentCooldowns)|TranslateRequestPair|CompatibilityRequestPair|ReverseRemapOAuthToolNamesRejectsUnsafeMangledAliases|RefreshTokensWithRetry_429BlocksImmediateReplay|Manager_RequestScopedErrorStopsCredentialFallbackWithoutSuspendingAuth)' ./sdk/cliproxy/auth ./internal/runtime/executor ./internal/auth/claude ./sdk/api/handlers/claude ./internal/runtime/executor/helps` — **5 packages / 20 top-level tests**, 15.06s. ### Remaining attachment/error-interface scope

Unsupported attachment-only turns can still lose their unrepresentable content under the existing behavior. This batch fixes the three representable-content losses above; it does **not** claim the upstream per-turn 400 rejection policy is implemented. There is no new file retrieval endpoint or local/remote URL fetch: supported URLs are forwarded as protocol fields for the selected upstream to consume.

A separate error-interface adaptation should define empty user turns independently of system/developer text and preceding/following turns; preserve ordinary text beside unrepresentable attachments; and propagate the **working payload's** translation error while allowing a repaired working request when only its baseline failed. Preserve independent request buffers and the local two-invocation contract for stateful plugins (upstream's helper now has different reuse conditions). Cover registry/pipeline/compatibility/multi-agent paths, HTTP/WebSocket/count_tokens and plugin executors before enabling refusals. Decide whether to retain public byte-returning compatibility wrappers or deliberately migrate the SDK API; include handler paths that currently discard converter errors. Required regressions include system-only input, blank text beside attachments, mixed/multiple user turns, tool results, summaries, plugin normalization and zero upstream calls on refusal. Keep this scoped separately from auth lifecycle, scheduling and storage migration.

All earlier deferred decisions remain in force, including explicit caching, credential versions/refresh, missing-thread recovery, tool-alias state, payload finalization and catalog/reporting frameworks. The October 7 OAuth/API-key placement fix, search-source fallback, EOF completion and compact SSE error handling remain intact.

### Protected lifecycle follow-up

Before selecting the deferred `50d5fe18` / `6920ed5d` / `935aa6e3` batch, discuss a plan that distinguishes rejected-token selection from existing 401, quota, capacity and explicit-disable state. Define when a monotonic credential version advances, including edit-and-revert, refresh, re-registration and persisted reload, and how legacy SDK results without a version are treated. Carry that version through execution results and refresh merges while preserving current registration/availability fencing, model-scoped cooldowns and user edits. Required regressions include late success/401, refresh during edit, ABA, disable/re-enable, cancellation, reload and concurrent execution. Keep the implementation and rollback scoped to auth manager/result/merge paths; no scheduler or storage migration is selected merely to follow upstream.

| [8ef43e4d](https://github.com/router-for-me/CLIProxyAPI/commit/8ef43e4df3b216a42493105d31c2873b69191473) | **Excluded upstream branding.** Sponsor README removal does not alter this fork documentation. |
| [1672be5d](https://github.com/router-for-me/CLIProxyAPI/commit/1672be5d25bdd84f360a49de3515a76ff2973e38) | **Deferred catalog configuration.** Adds source paths/URLs and hot reload across the unadopted v8 config layout, including explicit remote sources under `--local-model`. Preserve the fork's existing local-model behavior and pinned catalog. Revisit only with an agreed source precedence, offline behavior and reload/rollback contract. |
| [8a01bc02](https://github.com/router-for-me/CLIProxyAPI/commit/8a01bc022bf50a31bb8026f26eaa2fa786c60dd7) | **Partly equivalent; remaining lifecycle semantics await discussion.** `persistAuthLocked` already saves a clone outside the manager lock and uses per-credential persistence ordering, registration epochs and generations (`102f79aa`, `581aa6b9`). Upstream additionally introduces a cancelable whole-load mutation gate and reflects Store.Save-induced field deltas into live auth state. Those are not claimed equivalent or imported; the scoped proposal below covers concurrency and store ownership. |
| [c997cbb5](https://github.com/router-for-me/CLIProxyAPI/commit/c997cbb58afe0035fcb0cbd94aa766acb951c674) | **Partially adapted locally.** Add error-aware HTTP flushing through exact Gin and local logging/trace wrappers while preserving legacy buffered middleware. Responses initial/ongoing writes, short writes, heartbeats, terminal writes and flushes now cancel on transport failure. `ForwardStream` gains an optional error-aware flush callback. Exclude stream-delivery acknowledgments, plugin completion/accounting changes, Antigravity replay settlement and terminal-success early cancellation; retain explicit pending error precedence and the existing channel-close completion contract. |
| [5d890405](https://github.com/router-for-me/CLIProxyAPI/commit/5d890405b59c4b84a2f00ca39c4ee1494ce51a72) | **Integrated locally.** Keep caller system blocks top-level when the first consecutive user-message run reaches the end and would otherwise place a system turn last. Mirror count-tokens behavior; preserve strict, advisor, legacy reminder and payload reconciliation branches. |
| [26e5efbb](https://github.com/router-for-me/CLIProxyAPI/commit/26e5efbbdf6739eafa782cb98225e3fc2fbea99c) | **Integrated locally.** Reserve one cache breakpoint when a non-null Claude `thread` field exists and the limit is positive. Preserve cache marker removal order and TTL rules; no thread cache or continuation lifecycle is introduced. |
| [5c959cb0](https://github.com/router-for-me/CLIProxyAPI/commit/5c959cb032efb6f2067341915c9fb137532b8d5b) | **Awaiting request-error/cooldown discussion.** Upstream makes missing Claude thread state a replayable request-scoped 404 with `thread_not_found`, changing credential rotation/cooldown classification. Retain existing classification until the precise recognition and retry policy below is approved. |
| [678a9257](https://github.com/router-for-me/CLIProxyAPI/commit/678a92578ca346ff9e77d4811cbd41f899a8cc96) | **Deferred thread session state.** Adds a bounded in-memory message-ID-to-OAuth-tool-alias cache and request-scoped errors when continuation aliases are absent. Revisit together with missing-thread policy, account isolation, eviction, restart/multi-node behavior and replay expectations. Preserve existing strict request-local ambiguous/unresolved MCP alias guards. |
| [d31b61cb](https://github.com/router-for-me/CLIProxyAPI/commit/d31b61cb87c2664416368686c13f8bcd48e110a3) | **Partially integrated locally, Codex only.** Remember the current upstream connection's terminal read/binary error and return it if stream activation follows disconnect. Reset for replacement connections and reject stale readers; existing retry, prewarm, credential pinning and liveness rules remain. xAI code is excluded. |
| [4e4dede4](https://github.com/router-for-me/CLIProxyAPI/commit/4e4dede4811ab99c6ce20bdea3e45041e6304b29), [a4acc9f7](https://github.com/router-for-me/CLIProxyAPI/commit/a4acc9f752bd46571f737a10c04bf413656ab06b) | **Excluded Antigravity transport/settlement.** Backend-error/multiline-SSE support and post-terminal disconnect-success handling operate on the removed provider and its delivery bookkeeping. Native Responses transport propagation is recorded separately under `c997cbb5`. |
| [16d98881](https://github.com/router-for-me/CLIProxyAPI/commit/16d98881d4bb37adaa827599e4be8f5154e81646) | **Adapted locally with corrections to upstream.** Preserve Codex URL citations in Chat streaming/non-streaming responses and concatenate all output-text parts. Emit standard nested `url_citation` objects; compute character offsets from each part's start (including Unicode), deduplicate repeated events at their final positions, retain citations from terminal output, and reject malformed/negative/reversed/overflowing ranges. Do not copy upstream's flat annotation shape or current-end offset calculation, which can shift citations beyond their text. |
| [97dd9eec](https://github.com/router-for-me/CLIProxyAPI/commit/97dd9eec628c6b1e89847f31bb10c1da49b0402a) | **Integrated locally.** Chat/Completions streams that close after payload without any nonempty finish reason emit an error instead of `[DONE]`. Track the initial chunk as well as subsequent chunks, preserve explicit upstream error precedence, and retain normal finish/usage behavior. |

Citation adaptation follows the [official Chat message annotation schema](https://developers.openai.com/api/reference/resources/chat/subresources/completions/methods/create) and [OpenAI Python Chat message types](https://github.com/openai/openai-python/blob/main/src/openai/types/chat/chat_completion_message.py): URL fields belong inside `url_citation`, with character positions in the message. Tests cover repeated annotations, multiple parts/output items, Unicode and completion-only citation fallback.

### Deferred execution proposals

- **Auth persistence/mutation (`8a01bc02`):** first define which fields a Store.Save implementation may mutate and how those deltas merge without overwriting later refresh, edit, disabled or cooldown state. Keep per-ID persistence ordering and epoch/generation fencing. Assess a context-cancelable load gate separately; test canceled loads, concurrent save/edit/refresh, callback reentrancy, store errors and same-ID replacement. Rollback would revert only the chosen persistence/gate change and its tests; no stored format migration is proposed.
- **Missing-thread recovery (`5c959cb0`, `678a9257`):** limit recognition to Claude thread-bearing requests and verified missing-state responses, then decide whether replay is entirely client-owned and whether such failures affect any credential cooldown/rotation. If cross-request aliases are desired, define account-scoped cache keys, bounded eviction, restart/multi-node loss and full-history replay before adding state. Test unrelated 404s, authentication failures, concurrent credentials, missing tools and stale aliases. Roll back the classification and alias cache together without changing stored credentials.
- **Delivery settlement (excluded part of `c997cbb5`):** assess acknowledgment ownership and plugin/usage consumers before adding a delivery state framework or treating a terminal event as immediate success. Retain current late explicit-error behavior until the contract is decided. The selected write/flush error handling does not claim to settle end-to-end usage accounting.

Protected lifecycle/cooldown items remain discussion proposals under [the synchronization skill](/Users/vpromisex/.codex/skills/codexproxy-upstream-sync/SKILL.md): “Before changing existing cooldown, disable, scheduling, credential-refresh/lifecycle or migration semantics, discuss a concrete execution plan with the user unless that plan is already authorized in the active task.” Independent protocol repairs were completed without those changes.

### Proposed lifecycle follow-up, not implemented

- **Queue ownership (`81756a57`):** carry registration epoch and a job-owned pending marker through enqueue/start/finish, release abandoned queued jobs on loop shutdown, and avoid blocking the dispatcher on a full queue. Preserve the fork's disabled checks, five-day Codex lead, per-credential locks, generation/edit guards and existing backoff. Reproduce queue saturation, loop restart, disable/reenable, delete/recreate and concurrent refresh/edit cases before changing production code. Rollback would cover only the refresh-loop/job bookkeeping and matching tests, without stored credential migration.
- **Terminal refresh outcomes (`c163bae4`):** decide whether ambiguous transport/decode failures may retry a single-use Claude refresh token, and whether an explicitly rejected access token plus structured `invalid_grant` should stay unselectable until tokens change. Keep mere expiry/temporary errors distinct, retain valid-token and cooldown protections, and define force-refresh/manual recovery before implementation. Test late successful requests, manual cooldown reset, token replacement, concurrent refresh, 429/5xx versus transport errors, and same-ID recreation. Rollback must include the auth/refresh/cooldown code together; no filename/config migration is proposed.

These are discussion items under `/Users/vpromisex/.codex/skills/codexproxy-upstream-sync/SKILL.md`: “Before changing existing cooldown, disable, scheduling, credential-refresh/lifecycle or migration semantics, discuss a concrete execution plan with the user unless that plan is already authorized in the active task.” The independently selected protocol/catalog work is complete locally.

### Validation and exact inputs

Go **1.27.1**, **darwin/arm64**, **CGO_ENABLED=1**; dedicated temporary `GOCACHE` and `GOTMPDIR`, shared existing module cache, sanitized fixtures and local HTTP/TLS servers. No live inference, production credential loading, model updater run or server operation was used.

- Baseline production reproduced dropped Claude enabled effort, missing thinking replay after search, incorrect pause completion and missing Claude-to-Codex source inclusion. The initial four-package command exited 1; its source manifest was `6b84a655b57cfdaa6c322e119b55747556658453c9e83328cdaef9af146b5ce8` (**1572 files**). The upstream thinking test also initially referenced an absent v8 API; it was adapted to the existing `ApplyThinkingWithModelInfoAndSummary`, without adding a capability framework. That initial build failure is not counted as a thinking defect reproduction.
- A second old-stream-code run, `go test -count=1 -json -run 'TestCodex.*(Disconnect|Empty|Stream.*(Fail|Timeout))' ./test`, exited 1: both buffered/unbuffered empty streams and an abnormal pre-output disconnect failed to switch credentials; post-output behavior passed. Its manifest was `95216806d95143090c375effef13369db661c8ab57a5bfcda4c789836a1a3be25` (**1579 files**).
- Initial focused checks exposed two fixture-porting issues: the local error builder uses `BuildErrorResponseBody`, and upstream schema fixtures use broad `codex`/`Codex/1.0`/lowercase desktop user agents. Adapted builder calls/imports and used the already accepted `codex_cli_rs`/`Codex Desktop` identities for schema fixtures, retaining every schema assertion. An additional negative wrapper-UA test verifies the deliberately stricter local gate. No production behavior was weakened to satisfy a fixture.
- Focused validation completed with **11 packages passing** in the broad run (the schema package's fixture identities were corrected afterward); the final targeted repair run passed **6 packages / 26 top-level tests**, including integer schema shapes, explicit executor identity, real OpenAI-compatible HTTP forwarding, disconnect failover and error mapping. All selected packages subsequently passed the full and race runs below.

- `go test -race -count=1 -json -skip '^TestClaudeStandardRoundTripper(BindsLocalIP|HTTPSProxyKeepsBindAndProxyTLS)$' ./internal/thinking ./internal/translator/openai/claude ./internal/translator/claude/openai/responses ./internal/translator/codex/claude ./internal/translator/codex/openai/responses ./internal/client/codex/tool-schema ./internal/runtime/executor ./internal/runtime/executor/helps ./sdk/api/handlers ./sdk/api/handlers/claude ./sdk/api/handlers/openai ./sdk/cliproxy/auth ./internal/registry ./internal/client/codex/models ./test`: **15 packages / 2275 top-level tests passed**, exit **0**.
- `go build -o <task-directory>/test-output ./cmd/server`: passed, exit **0**. `gofmt` and `git diff --check` passed; unrelated preexisting formatting was restored. Final diff review retained local provider routes, canonical thinking, native prewarm/session handling, strict MCP ambiguity checks, cooldown/refresh policy and existing catalogs/instructions.

A fresh socket bind confirmed macOS errno **49 / EADDRNOTAVAIL** for `127.0.0.2`; the same two historical command-level exclusions apply. `TestClaudeCodeTLSClientHelloCapture` skipped itself because `CPA_TLS_FP_PROXY` was unset. ## Authorized local adaptation on 2026-09-29 — UUID and native refresh

The user requested implementation of complete UUID support, selection of refresh changes against the current platform, and plugin fixes only if the current version actually uses those paths. All other deferred families remain deferred. This batch follows the preceding concrete proposal and preserves the earlier four uncommitted fixes; it is not a full upstream merge. The reviewed upstream boundary remains **v8.0.4 / `d33f63f8e3d98428440ebca5a5b6a981a61ff71e`**.

### Current configuration and selected scope

A read-only, whitelisted inspection of the local `config.yaml` found **`plugins.enabled=false`**, **0 configured plugin instances**, no custom plugin directory and no Home section. The default `plugins/` directory contains **0 files**. No config contents, credentials or secrets were printed, copied into tests or changed. `Host.ApplyConfig` publishes an empty plugin snapshot when disabled; `Service.registerExecutorForAuth` selects native Codex/Claude executors, and normal native file synthesis/management mutations do not invoke `HostAuthSave` or the plugin refresh adapters. The builtin Redis usage plugin is a different usage-accounting interface and does not activate plugin credential handling. Therefore **`5756d76e` and the plugin portions of `5530cbd2` are not synchronized**. This establishes the current local configuration/code path, not a live audit of dmit/dmit2/bawg; no remote server or production credential directory was accessed.

| Batch | Implemented behavior | Retained boundaries |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Refresh scheduling, adapting `58461c8f` | `nextRefreshCheckAt`, `shouldRefresh` and `markRefreshPending` honor both `Disabled` and `StatusDisabled`, matching the existing worker rule. Dirty scheduling removes disabled entries without queued jobs or generation changes; explicit reenable schedules again. | Keep refresh locks, registration epochs, concurrent edit/delete/recreate protection, valid-token retention and monotonic cooldowns. No new refresh counter, unschedule/dirty-map race, or general scheduling redesign. |
| Native token errors, related local repair | Codex refresh HTTP errors retain status and parse the OAuth error code from either the standard string or nested `error.code`. A confirmed HTTP 400/401 `invalid_grant` stops immediate token retries. Claude's existing refresh error exposes its status through wrappers. | Preserve Codex `refresh_token_reused`, transient/malformed/429/5xx retry behavior, Claude nonretryable/429 protection, current five-minute background retry and unauthorized-expired-token stopping. No status-less global substring broadening or 1–30/5–30 minute exponential policy. |
| Complete UUIDv7, adapting `d33f63f8` | Complete IDs in Gin/context, application logs, request/error/stream filenames, management lookup, usage queue and CPA trace headers. A shared filename splitter preserves the complete ID around numeric collision suffixes and management ordering. UUID entropy failure is logged without inventing a shared fallback ID or blocking inference. | Keep exclusive 0600 log creation, old eight-character IDs and no event-deduplication changes. Exact lookup rejects partial UUID components; two UUIDs with equal trailing characters remain distinct. No `ShortRequestID` truncation. |
| Management UI integration | Bracketed and pipe-delimited UUID log lines retain the complete request ID, level/source/status fields and old-ID compatibility. Rebuild the embedded management page from locked dependencies using the existing version placeholder. | No API/UI feature expansion, styling redesign, runtime fingerprint or catalog refresh. |

The existing `X-CPA-TRACE-ID` layout remains `timestamp-authIndex-requestID`; consumers must split only the first two separators because the final request ID now contains hyphens. The inspected local Keeper source passes full request IDs through URL escaping and has no eight-character storage restriction. No Keeper file was edited and no deployed Keeper compatibility is claimed. v8 configuration/module migration, Home capabilities, plugin quota/scheduler extensions, token-plan defaulting and watcher replacement-delay changes remain deferred.

## Deferred-family reassessment on 2026-09-29 — analysis only

The user requested a deeper value assessment of v8 migration, refresh policy, Home/plugin extensions and UUID request IDs. This review reuses the immutable **v8.0.4 / `d33f63f8e3d98428440ebca5a5b6a981a61ff71e`** boundary; it does not claim a newer upstream-main check. The local base remains `67798d3c919720bd77c10db3ce2b0685a81d6e5c` with the previously selected, uncommitted September 29 fixes. Only this ledger changed in the main checkout during the reassessment. Recommendations below are not integrated behavior and do not authorize publication or deployment.

### Recommended narrow changes and preservation contracts

1. **Plugin auth-save disabled state — `5756d76e`: worthwhile correctness fix.** `internal/pluginhost/auth_callbacks.go` constructs active auth records without reading `disabled`; the normal native file-synthesis path does not intervene. A temp-file fixture using the real file store reproduced both initial save and update with `disabled:true` ending in `Disabled=false`, `Status=active` and on-disk `disabled:false`. Disk-only listings also lose disabled/status information. Adapt the metadata parsing and entry construction only. Validate initial save, explicit disable, explicit reenable, disk-only listing and ordinary native synthesis. Clarify absent-field behavior rather than silently introducing a new disable policy. Impact requires a plugin host callback; no deployed plugin use was inspected or established.
2. **Plugin refresh attribute/path preservation — relevant parts of `5530cbd2`: worthwhile correctness fix.** Both `Host.RefreshAuth` and `executorAdapter.Refresh` copy old attributes only when the returned map is completely empty. A plugin returning just `priority` therefore drops `path`, `source_backend` and other unchanged attributes; conversion replaces `source` with the bare filename. File and Postgres-source fixtures reproduce this. A separate fixture calls the real `Manager.UpdateRefreshedAuth`: the local three-way merge interprets omitted unchanged attributes as deletion, so it does not repair the loss. The existing `pluginRefreshCompatExecutor` makes this relevant to explicitly enabled plugin credentials using OpenAI compatibility, without adding a new native provider. Merge omitted attributes before conversion, preserve original backend/path ownership, allow explicit replacement values, and retain manager protection for concurrent edits/deletion/recreation. Do not introduce upstream's separate management refresh endpoint as a dependency.
3. **Disabled refresh scheduling — adapt the intent of `58461c8f` to the fork.** Here, `refreshAuthForRequest` already rejects every disabled credential, but `nextRefreshCheckAt`, `shouldRefresh` and `markRefreshPending` do not consistently enforce that decision. Three simulated due periods generated **3 queued jobs, 0 upstream refresh calls, and generation 1 → 4**. Add matching disabled checks at scheduling/enqueue boundaries and remove stale heap entries, while preserving the worker guard. This saves unnecessary scheduling/state changes; it is not a claim that disabled credentials currently make token requests. Test disable before enqueue, disable during refresh, reenable/reschedule, credential replacement and same-ID recreation. The misleading existing `TestNextRefreshCheckAt_DisabledUnschedule` currently asserts that the credential *is* scheduled. Upstream permits ordinary disabled auths to refresh and only excludes disabled `invalid_grant`; copying its whole policy would weaken the fork's existing contract.
4. **Invalid-grant handling — useful separate refresh proposal, not a verbatim backport.** Native Codex refresh errors are plain errors containing the HTTP status; the existing classifier requires a `StatusCode`-style value and misses `token refresh failed with status 400: {"error":"invalid_grant"}`. A fake HTTP transport also reproduced **three token requests** for that response in one `RefreshTokensWithRetry(..., 3)` invocation (the current nonretryable exception only recognizes `refresh_token_reused`). Prefer retaining structured status/OAuth error codes from the token endpoint and suppressing immediate retries for a confirmed invalid grant. That inner retry repair is derived from local evidence, not implemented by `58461c8f`. Keep transient-error behavior and valid access tokens. If exponential scheduling is desired, propose **5 → 10 → 20 → 30 minutes** for repeated confirmed invalid grants, with counters reset after success/credential replacement and retries clamped to access-token expiry where applicable. Upstream's **1 → 2 → 4 → 8 → 16 → 30** starts more aggressively than the local five-minute retry. Preserve unauthorized-expired-token stopping, registration epochs, concurrent cooldowns and manual disable; do not extend a broad substring match to unrelated errors without tests.
5. **UUIDv7 — `d33f63f8`: worthwhile after adapting the complete local log path.** The current ID contains 32 random bits. With uniform independent IDs, the probability of at least one collision among 10,000 IDs is about **1.16%**, and among 100,000 about **68.8%**; these are collision-domain probabilities, not measured production collision rates or percentages of affected requests. Full UUIDs improve request/log correlation. Upstream's last-eight-character filenames and lookup retain a 32-bit ambiguity, so omit that truncation from the lookup/storage path. A direct full-UUID filename fixture exposed another integration dependency: `createUniqueLogFile` splits at the last hyphen and inserts `_1` *inside* the UUID on a repeated filename, breaking complete-ID suffix matching. `parseLogMetadata` also assumes a hyphen-free trailing ID. Adapt both with the filename writer and lookup, preserve exclusive file creation and legacy eight-character log lookup, and test same-ID multi-file logs, two UUIDs sharing their last eight characters, tie ordering, streaming/error logs and entropy-failure handling. `google/uuid` is already a dependency. `GenerateRequestID` is in `internal/logging`, not an exported SDK API; the earlier signature concern was overstated.
6. **Watcher retry bundled in `5530cbd2`: a separate, lower-priority resilience option.** The synthetic rename fixture makes a known Codex auth file visible after 60 ms; the current single 50 ms check removes its hash/runtime entry before the replacement is visible. Upstream adds up to three 25 ms retries for known files, extending the grace window to roughly 125 ms. This can prevent transient removal during delayed file visibility, but the fixture does not demonstrate a real operating-system atomic-rename failure or permanent disappearance after subsequent create events. It also delays genuine deletions and blocks the event loop longer. If selected, test true deletion, delayed replacement, unknown files, disable/recreate races and subsequent create events separately; do not assume the plugin adapter repair requires this watcher policy change.

### Keep v8 and Home feature migration deferred

The v8 management API offers useful field-level GET/PUT/PATCH/DELETE operations, grouped provider keys with inheritance/explicit overrides, organized configuration sections and OAuth-only options isolated from API-key requests. These are management/product benefits; no required local caller or blocking configuration defect was established. [Upstream's versioned API documentation](https://github.com/router-for-me/CLIProxyAPI/blob/d33f63f8e3d98428440ebca5a5b6a981a61ff71e/docs/management-api-v8.md) and the migrator show that `/v0/management` remains supported and legacy-only YAML keeps its layout until a successful v8 configuration write. Reads do not migrate files; mixed-layout conflict normalization can remove legacy equivalents. Thus the earlier claim that the UI, SDK and module all require simultaneous migration was too broad. The `/v8` Go module rename is separable from the configuration API.

A future migration still needs an explicit rollout: retain v0 alongside filtered v8 routes, snapshot configuration before the first migration-triggering write, test old/new/mixed YAML and false/null/zero inheritance, retain comment/unknown-field handling, and restore the legacy configuration with an old-binary rollback. Old code does not acquire support for the nested layout merely because v0 remains available in the new code. The OAuth-scope filter is for new v8-scoped fields and intentionally retains legacy global semantics; it is not proof of an existing legacy API-key regression. Do not re-expose Vertex, Antigravity, Kimi, xAI or other removed native routes. Existing configuration comment preservation is already local; the unknown-field archive fixes are tied to the new migrator, not a necessary standalone upgrade.

`b9971ee3` / `ac42accc` make the selected Home credential's `credential_options` and routed model authoritative for compatibility, prompt-cache support, max-completion-token conversion and tool-result normalization. These matter if a Home control plane supplies those options; no such deployment/consumer was established. They interact with the deferred resolved-capability work (`ed980be3`) and must preserve canonical thinking and suffix precedence. Local direct-provider compatibility options should remain unchanged. The public quota wrappers are independently separable *upstream*, but this fork has neither the corresponding `sdk/pluginapi` quota request types nor the internal plugin host quota operations, so copying the small wrapper file would not be a standalone working fix. `6cd86fb2` adds an explicit terminal scheduler rejection contract; useful if an enabled scheduler needs to deny selection instead of falling back, but no such local consumer was identified. Keep these extensions deferred until that requirement exists.

### Correction: Keeper identity semantics

Read-only inspection of sibling **CodexKeeper `f7ea9f75b56abb904d7dbedbcb19f3158928fde5`** found that `internal/service/redis_usage.go:toUsageEvent` assigns `request_id` to `event_key`, but `internal/repository/db.go:InsertUsageEvents` deliberately inserts duplicate IDs as separate records. Migration `20260514_usage_event_event_key_index.go` removes the old unique index; `20260610_remove_usage_event_write_heavy_indexes.go` later removes the nonunique index too. `UsageEvent.RequestID` and `EventKey` are strings without an eight-character restriction. Therefore prior references to Keeper deduplicating by request ID were incorrect for this inspected source. This corrects the logging/trace-ID rationale, not published implementation or deployment history. No production Keeper binary/database was inspected.

Keeper's request-log service does use the complete request ID to fetch logs and to coalesce concurrent fetches. UUIDs therefore have a concrete correlation benefit without requiring an event-deduplication change. Existing usage-retention semantics should remain intact. Any later implementation must verify the actual deployed consumer version; this source review is not a live compatibility test.

### Diagnostic evidence and limits

An isolated detached worktree reproduced the main checkout's **1562-file** source/test/build manifest `915df6569b6e85167be30608ffa204f96250bdbb8ceb868bceb655163f7f4aa3` (excluding this ledger), then added **five test-only files**. No production source was modified in that worktree. Upstream plugin regressions were adapted only to v7 imports and diagnostic names; nonfatal assertions allowed observation of both runtime and persisted state. The watcher fixture uses a supported Codex provider. Additional local probes exercise the real manager merge/scheduler, a fake Codex HTTP transport and full-UUID filename collisions. These are local fixtures with fabricated credentials; there was no provider request, production auth-file access or server operation.

```bash
go test -count=1 -v -run '^TestDeferredReview' ./internal/pluginhost ./sdk/cliproxy/auth ./internal/logging ./internal/auth/codex ./internal/watcher
```

Go **1.27.1**, darwin/arm64, **CGO_ENABLED=1** compiled all five diagnostic packages. The command exited **1**, with **10 top-level diagnostic tests failing at the intended behavior/compatibility assertions**; there were no build failures or skipped tests. These failures establish the observations above, not validation of a completed fix. The UUID test checks a proposed future full-ID contract; the watcher test checks a simulated delayed-visibility scenario. An earlier four-package run reproduced the same core observations before adding the watcher and allowing later plugin-save assertions to execute. No full-suite/race/server-build claim is made for this analysis-only turn; prior completed synchronization validation remains recorded separately below.

Final diagnostic input manifest: **1567 files**, `4e4cd4f7d87ca998f94388b7833cbaf94a13af36b12ccfd65dce73f4aff3892e`. The small test-only patch and final log are retained outside the repository under `/private/tmp/codexproxy-deferred-20260929.zg2YJc/` for follow-up: `diagnostics.patch` SHA-256 **`a4344421610a4010aff147c51426c7a086c0c4f07df1a8d6252c3697b47cca43`**, `diagnostics-final.log` SHA-256 **`12b4953f9f7e6ce4b04181459342fbd71003f919eda620d986ec766ed8883049`**. Temporary worktree and dedicated caches were removed after preserving these diagnostics. The main source manifest remains unchanged. ### Validation and local integration

The new regression cases failed against unchanged production source: four Claude tool-change alias cases, the blank Codex arguments case, namespace collision/tool loss, and two complete-frame assertions. An initial fixture run had a missing `bytes` test import; after correcting that test-only setup, all four affected baseline packages ran and failed on the intended assertions. The corrected baseline manifest was `1cd77c0287a9bfc1f1466779843ee9d41a2b84508e21534afb4c4f72f0b56ad2` (**1561** regular inputs). No production code was changed for those failures.

Final verification used Go **1.27.1**, darwin/arm64, **CGO_ENABLED=1**, dedicated temporary worktree/build/test caches and local fixtures. `gofmt -w .` was run; unrelated formatting in one pre-existing replay test was restored before final validation. No behavioral assertion was weakened and no upstream digest was repinned.

All completed commands below exited 0:

- Focused checks: `go test -count=1 -json -skip '^TestClaudeStandardRoundTripper(BindsLocalIP|HTTPSProxyKeepsBindAndProxyTLS)$' ./internal/runtime/executor ./internal/runtime/executor/helps ./internal/translator/codex/openai/responses ./internal/translator/openai/openai/responses ./internal/translator/claude/openai/responses ./internal/translator/common ./sdk/api/handlers/openai`: **7 packages, 1345 top-level tests passed**. This initial run included the unrelated formatting-only change removed before the final full/race runs.
- Full suite: `go test -count=1 -json -skip '^TestClaudeStandardRoundTripper(BindsLocalIP|HTTPSProxyKeepsBindAndProxyTLS)$' ./...`: **67 packages, 3707 top-level tests passed**; **24 packages had no tests**.
- Race checks: `go test -race -count=1 -json -skip '^TestClaudeStandardRoundTripper(BindsLocalIP|HTTPSProxyKeepsBindAndProxyTLS)$' ./internal/runtime/executor ./internal/runtime/executor/helps ./internal/translator/codex/openai/responses ./internal/translator/openai/openai/responses ./internal/translator/claude/openai/responses ./internal/translator/common ./sdk/api/handlers/openai ./sdk/cliproxy/auth`: **8 packages, 2011 top-level tests passed**. The three existing WebSocket allocation-budget tests skipped under race instrumentation and all passed in the ordinary full suite.
- `go build -o <task-directory>/test-output ./cmd/server`: passed. Final diff review and `git diff --check` passed.

A fresh local bind to `127.0.0.2` failed with errno 49 (`EADDRNOTAVAIL`), confirming the existing two command-level exclusions. The optional `TestClaudeCodeTLSClientHelloCapture` skipped because its external capture proxy was not configured. No new environment exclusion was added. This is local macOS fixture coverage, not Linux validation or live provider inference.

The final source/test/build manifest is **`915df6569b6e85167be30608ffa204f96250bdbb8ceb868bceb655163f7f4aa3`**, covering **1562** regular files excluding this ledger, using the existing sorted SHA-256/path manifest algorithm documented below. The final patch has **5 production paths and 4 test paths**, plus this ledger. Source inputs matched before and after final checks and after exact application to the unchanged main-checkout base. The index remains empty. Changes are local and uncommitted; no push, tag, Release or deployment occurred. Disposable worktree, binary, logs and dedicated caches were removed after recording this evidence; the shared module cache and user-owned files were retained.

## Published and deployed on 2026-09-26

The user explicitly requested pushing to GitHub and deployment to dmit, dmit2 and bawg. The exact 15 selected paths were committed in [6e493aeb](https://github.com/vpromise/CodexProxy/commit/6e493aebddff1a476dbe509cc26614ab9d5b0e71) and pushed normally to `vpromise/CodexProxy:main`. GitHub's branch and commit APIs confirmed SHA `6e493aebddff1a476dbe509cc26614ab9d5b0e71` and tree `ecca59ff220fb55d0c62f57b9e4f6443c18d61d3`. The **1561-file** input manifest still matched `6360028e807f5e362e297a7b2baabdfd9acdbe88e116977274c00a42a554bbf6`, allowing reuse of the recorded 3702-test full check, 1721-test race check and successful compile. No runtime code changed during publication or deployment.

Artifacts were built from a detached, clean worktree at that published SHA, without refreshing catalogs or rebuilding the unchanged bundled UI. Both builds used Go **1.27.1**, `GOOS=linux`, `GOARCH=amd64`, `-trimpath`, `-buildvcs=false` and embedded version `codexproxy-20260926-6e493aeb`, the full source SHA and build time `2026-09-26T03:26:34Z`. The CGO variant used a checksum-verified official Zig **0.15.2** compiler targeting `x86_64-linux-gnu.2.17`, matching the prior deployment toolchain. Go build metadata and ELF format were inspected; each artifact passed a native Linux `--help` version smoke check before switching its target. These are artifact/startup checks, not a claim that the full test suite ran on Linux.

### Server rollout and verification

All targets previously ran `8bf1f13c090a31a4f5c1b7049d3a719828674903`; the rollout therefore includes the already published September 24 logging/citation/cooldown repairs as well as the September 25 selection. Servers were switched and verified sequentially. Existing configuration files, management passwords, credentials, data mounts, Docker networks and systemd units were preserved. Only CodexProxy was restarted; Keeper and the other application services retained their process/container identity and start time.

| Target | Existing deployment method retained | Final state | Rollback and evidence directory |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| dmit | Docker Compose `cpa-production` / `cpa`; CGO binary; image `local/codexproxy:6e493aeb` | Running, Docker healthy, zero automatic restarts | `/root/cpa-deploy/releases/20260926-6e493aeb/` |
| dmit2 | Docker Compose `codex-stack` / `codexproxy`; static binary; image `local/codexproxy:6e493aeb` | Running, HTTP checks passed, zero automatic restarts; Docker healthcheck remains unconfigured | `/opt/codex-stack/codexproxy/releases/20260926-6e493aeb/` |
| bawg | Existing `codexproxy.service`; static `/opt/codexproxy/codexproxy` | Active/running, zero automatic restarts | `/opt/codexproxy/releases/20260926-6e493aeb/` |

At **03:42 UTC / 11:42 Asia/Shanghai**, all three running binaries matched the artifact checksums above and exposed commit `6e493aebddff1a476dbe509cc26614ab9d5b0e71` in management response headers. Local health returned **200**, Claude connection warmup returned **204**, unauthenticated inference-model listing returned **401**, and the management page returned **200**. Configuration hashes and unrelated services matched their pre-deployment state; startup logs contained zero warnings/errors. No live provider inference was issued.

The existing reverse proxies also passed checks. Public HTTPS probes with certificate validation returned **200** for [dmit management](https://cpa.vpromise.fun/management.html), [dmit2 health](https://proxy.vpromise.fun/healthz) and [bawg health](https://cpa.hoperouter.com/healthz). dmit intentionally exposes management/keeper/callback routes on its public host, while `/healthz` and `/v1` remain unavailable there; its internal health/API checks used loopback. Its local TLS listener uses an origin certificate, so that loopback-only probe bypassed certificate-chain verification; the separate public HTTPS probe verified the public certificate normally. No reverse-proxy route, TLS configuration or access rule was changed.

Two deployment-tool compatibility issues were handled and recorded:

- dmit's first switched attempt passed health checks, then automatically rolled back because Docker reordered otherwise identical environment variables and mount bindings. Comparing those unordered lists by content preserved all values/paths; the subsequent deployment and final verification succeeded. The failed attempt remains in `deployment-attempt1.json` alongside rollback evidence.
- dmit2's first image-build attempt stopped before service switching because its legacy Docker builder does not support `COPY --chmod`. Setting the source file mode to 0755 and using ordinary `COPY` built the same verified binary successfully. The failure and successful attempt are recorded separately; the Docker daemon/tooling was not upgraded.

Each server retains `BUILD.json`, `deployment.json`, `FINAL_VERIFICATION.json`, `PUBLIC_VERIFICATION.json`, a protected configuration snapshot, the prior binary/image and executable `ROLLBACK.sh`. The deployment root's `LATEST_DEPLOYMENT.json` points to the successful version and final verification. The Docker images were built from the exact previously running local image to preserve their runtime base; no mutable registry pull was needed. Temporary local worktree, downloaded compiler, archives, binaries, dedicated caches and verification logs were removed after recording this evidence. The user's duplicate-source backup remains intact outside the repository.

### Approved cooldown repair and combined validation

After discussing the scope, the user explicitly requested the cooldown repair on September 24. A fresh isolated worktree included all earlier pending logging/translator/ledger changes. Manager regressions on unchanged cooldown code reproduced both the same-model and cross-model eight-day-to-three-hour contamination, inherited model backoff, lost credential backoff progression, and healthy-model rejection after the shared window elapsed. That baseline manifest was `d7569bd0918a6012ecb3ac361dbe6c106c0c5a3564eb9188cd616b8fdd453a28` (**1555** files). A separate executor regression also failed in both `Execute` and `ExecuteStream`: a local HTTP fixture returned a model-only eight-day 429 followed by a shared three-hour 429, but the manager recorded an eight-day credential deadline. Its unchanged-production baseline manifest was `e835b0eccc63acb4aaab333c8cc6d6df92abf6e0760e93ad7978eee88aa4a360` (**1556** files). Both baseline commands exited 1 as expected.

The adaptation changes only `sdk/cliproxy/auth/conductor_cooldown.go` in production and adds three test paths, including the expanded monotonic regressions. It retains the local `applyCooldownFields` behavior instead of replacing quota records wholesale. A genuine shared rejection still blocks all models for its own duration; after that window, healthy models become selectable while the independently restricted model retains its longer deadline. No existing recorded deadline is migrated or cleared, including any credential cooldown previously contaminated by the old implementation. Rollback consists of reverting these four cooldown-specific paths; no schema or configuration rollback is needed.

Validation used Go **1.27.1**, darwin/arm64, **CGO_ENABLED=1**, dedicated temporary build/test caches and local fixtures. All commands completed with exit code 0:

Deployed the immutable published source [8bf1f13c](https://github.com/vpromise/CodexProxy/commit/8bf1f13c090a31a4f5c1b7049d3a719828674903), tree `aa64c546526d726a7fc70cb664c01e120cb9a9d2`, including implementation `2067d9f3`. Each host passed executable preflight with an empty config before activation. Docker images retained their existing runtime base, with the compiled binary replaced. Only the proxy service was recreated or restarted, in order: dmit, dmit2, bawg. On dmit2, the legacy builder rejected `COPY --chmod` during image preparation; the existing service remained running. Retrying with an executable source file and ordinary `COPY` succeeded, without changing the binary, Docker installation or service settings. Its preparation failure remains recorded in `deployment-attempt1.json`. The persistent dmit2 image-build binary was also updated and its previous copy retained.

Final verification at **03:39 UTC** found all three services running with **zero automatic restarts**, `/healthz` returning **200**, and `HEAD /api/hello` returning **204**. dmit's configured Docker healthcheck was healthy; dmit2 has no Docker healthcheck, so its running process and HTTP endpoints were checked directly. Gateway-to-proxy checks passed from sub2api on dmit and NewAPI on dmit2. Config hashes, Docker runtime settings, bawg's systemd unit and unrelated gateway/service identities were preserved. Logs since activation contained **zero warnings and zero errors** at verification. No real provider inference request was sent; these checks verify deployment and connectivity, not end-to-end inference behavior.

Each host's `LATEST_DEPLOYMENT.json` and release-local `deployment.json` contain the build identifiers, checksums, before/after state and final verification. Release directories retain `BUILD.json`, config snapshots, the previous image reference or binary, and executable `ROLLBACK.sh`; rollback targets `7c96a1cc`. No old release or rollback material was deleted, and no GitHub Release was created. Local disposable source, compiler, binaries and dedicated caches were removed after preserving the deployment evidence.

The old exact 2.1.258 helper software tuple remains accepted alongside the new default tuple; foreign SDK/runtime combinations are still rejected for the helper exception. Existing body, session and exact helper-beta checks remain strict. This is not a blanket recognition expansion to unmeasured helper shapes.

No credential refresh/lifecycle, cooldown deadlines, warning-versus-exhaustion, disabled-state, scheduler, proxy routing, timeout, provider/configuration/dependency surface or deployment behavior changed.

### Validation and publication state

The unchanged production baseline failed the new Responses-string, required-null-option and 2.1.280 beta regressions, while the upstream credential-quota regression and existing model-projection tests passed. Baseline plus these first regression inputs: **1548** regular files, manifest SHA-256 `17050f9923b2f05e9bd841337e74178b3277ead9ae55a2142d609ab0c64e205d`.

The final candidate uses Go **1.27.1**, macOS arm64, **CGO_ENABLED=1**, an isolated worktree and dedicated temporary Go build/test caches. Focused request/header/model regressions passed. After the final model-beta gating adjustment, `go test -count=1 -json -skip '^TestClaudeStandardRoundTripper(BindsLocalIP|HTTPSProxyKeepsBindAndProxyTLS)$' ./...` completed with exit code 0: **67** packages passed and **24** had no tests. `go build -o <task-directory>/test-output ./cmd/server` completed with exit code 0. Formatting, `git diff --check`, pinned-catalog comparisons and final source review passed.

Race validation covered `internal/runtime/executor`, `internal/runtime/executor/helps`, `internal/translator/claude/openai/responses`, `internal/client/codex/models`, `internal/registry`, `internal/api` and `sdk/api/handlers/openai`. The final combined `go test -race -count=1 -json` command used the same two named exclusions: **6** packages passed and the OpenAI handler package failed once at `TestResponsesWebsocketReplaysImmediatelyAfterPinnedAuthFailure/rate_limit_to_websocket`. It observed close **1006 / unexpected EOF**, rather than **1012 / upstream requires HTTP replay**. This was an assertion failure, with no race-detector data-race report; do not describe that combined run as fully passing.

Initial follow-up ran the unchanged local base and the final candidate in separate worktrees. On **each** tree, `go test -race -count=50 -json -run '^TestResponsesWebsocketReplaysImmediatelyAfterPinnedAuthFailure$' ./sdk/api/handlers/openai` passed all 50 repetitions, and `go test -race -count=10 -json ./sdk/api/handlers/openai` passed all 10 package repetitions, with exit code 0. These checks did not reproduce the failure or establish a fix. The WebSocket implementation, its test and auth scheduling code were byte-identical to the base; the upstream batch only changed catalog serialization in the OpenAI handler package. The initial `writeMu.TryLock()` hypothesis was unconfirmed and is superseded by the channel-ordering reproduction and repair recorded below. The original failing combined run remains a failed run; the later verified repair has its own source snapshot and results.

The two command-level exclusions were reconfirmed by a local bind to `127.0.0.2` failing with `EADDRNOTAVAIL`. `TestClaudeCodeTLSClientHelloCapture` skipped because `CPA_TLS_FP_PROXY` was unset. Race instrumentation intentionally skipped three WebSocket allocation-budget tests (`TestMergeResponsesWebsocketInputBoundsLargeTranscriptAllocations`, `TestNormalizeResponseSubsequentRequestBoundsTranscriptAllocations`, `TestResponsesWebsocketFallbackTurnBoundsTranscriptAllocations`); they passed in the ordinary full suite. No Linux run, live inference call or server operation was performed.

The upstream-batch input manifest SHA-256 was `d831f1d69fdfa6983fc44f88f57f0fa9a88b9723fa06b2db986db0156d251012`, covering **1550** tracked or non-ignored untracked regular files, excluding only this ledger. Use the manifest algorithm recorded for the September 22 batch. Inputs matched before and after those checks and after integration into the unchanged local base. The pinned Codex client catalog SHA-256 is `7b15fec55ed279c2a0f4b6dfd1f7d2617d7c9f22e94d385242a8cd9534411d38`. Production/tool Go changes in that batch touch eight existing files plus one request-feature helper; the two JSON catalogs carry the model-data update. These changes were subsequently published with the WebSocket repair in `2067d9f3`; no Release or deployment was performed. Both temporary worktrees, build output, scratch logs and dedicated caches for that batch were removed after local integration and snapshot verification. The shared module cache was retained. The subsequent WebSocket repair changes the current tested snapshot as recorded next.

### Follow-up: WebSocket terminal-error ordering on 2026-09-23

The close-code investigation reproduced a preexisting logical race in `sdk/api/handlers/openai/openai_responses_websocket_forward.go`. `ExecuteStreamWithAuthManager` publishes a terminal error to its buffered error channel before closing the error and data channels. If the consumer starts or resumes after both are ready, Go's `select` can receive the data-channel close first. The old WebSocket forwarder immediately synthesized `408 / stream closed before response.completed` and hard-closed the connection, leaving the actual 401/429 error unread. This bypassed the existing pinned-credential replay handling, so the client observed **1006 / unexpected EOF** instead of **1012 / upstream requires HTTP replay**. The same omission could discard actionable request errors or a mapped message-too-big close. This is an event-ordering defect; it does not require a memory data race or an active data writer.

Evidence and scope:

- A local WebSocket regression with the error already buffered and both channels closed failed on both the unchanged `2a6daac1` production baseline and the upstream candidate, reproducing 1006 and the substituted 408 for both 401 and 429. The initial reproducer file SHA-256 was `cdd1fa750ba00ebd75fc2369c7c88c31bb7721dc738980d6e4233689435e4501`.
- A temporary barrier in the isolated baseline let the fixture's error producer finish before forwarding its continuation. The **unchanged original replay test** then failed in **15 of 40 subcases** across 10 repetitions; all 15 failures logged a closed data channel with one error still buffered. With only the proposed forwarder repair applied and the same barrier retained, all **40 subcases** passed. The barrier and diagnostic logging were investigation-only and were not integrated. No sleep, retry, new exclusion or assertion weakening was used.
- The repair checks the existing nonblocking `handlers.PendingStreamError` helper before treating data-channel closure as EOF or success, and reuses the existing terminal-error branch. It changes one production file and adds one regression-test file. The writer/close policy, credential selection, cooldown, disabled state, lifecycle and provider boundaries remain unchanged. The original fixture has serialized writes and no upstream-disconnect subscriber; writer contention is not needed to explain this reproduction. Other intentional hard-close paths can still report 1006.

Validation of the combined upstream batch and local repair used Go **1.27.1**, macOS arm64, **CGO_ENABLED=1**, isolated worktrees and dedicated temporary caches:

- `go test -race -count=20 -json -run '^(TestForwardResponsesWebsocketPreservesPendingErrorOnClose|TestResponsesWebsocketReplaysImmediatelyAfterPinnedAuthFailure|TestResponsesWebsocketWriterCloseDoesNotWaitForActiveDataWriter|TestResponsesWebsocketGenericDisconnectDoesNotWaitForActiveDataWriter)$' ./sdk/api/handlers/openai` passed. The new regression exercises 401/429 replay, 413-to-1009 mapping, an exposed 400 request error and a silent 502 failure, with 32 ready-channel trials per case per repetition (**3,200** trials total). It also checks preservation of the original error and exactly one cancellation.
- `go test -count=1 -json -skip '^TestClaudeStandardRoundTripper(BindsLocalIP|HTTPSProxyKeepsBindAndProxyTLS)$' ./...` passed: **67** packages passed and **24** had no tests.
- `go test -race -count=1 -json` with the same two exclusions passed all **9** packages: the seven from the upstream-batch race check plus `sdk/api/handlers` and `sdk/cliproxy/auth`. No race-detector report occurred.
- `go build -o <task-directory>/test-output ./cmd/server`, formatting and `git diff --check` passed. The local bind to `127.0.0.2` again failed with `EADDRNOTAVAIL`, justifying the two existing exclusions. The optional TLS capture and three race-only allocation-budget skips remain as described above; ordinary allocation tests passed. There was no Linux run, live inference call or server operation.

The final combined input manifest SHA-256 is **`99c53d4af79932f6dde59d9273c3c905e17f641e021fd49603255e4c81d8f16b`**, covering **1551** tracked or non-ignored untracked regular files, excluding only this ledger. Source inputs matched before and after validation, after local integration and before publication. All earlier pending changes were preserved. Both investigation worktrees, temporary probes, binary, logs and dedicated caches were removed after preserving these results; the shared module cache was retained. This repair is committed and published in `2067d9f3`; no Release or deployment was performed.

## Published on 2026-09-22 — selected v7.3.12 updates

The following four groups are published in [6b6d721f](https://github.com/vpromise/CodexProxy/commit/6b6d721f33ed274a4dc28664eb62b6d152409fc7), based on `f6795e4fa55d57f62de301cfadccc7f23efaf8b3`: six production/data paths, four test/benchmark paths and this ledger. Publication did not deploy the server.

| Selected group | Upstream source | Included behavior and preservation boundary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Codex SSE allocation | [cbf83183](https://github.com/router-for-me/CLIProxyAPI/commit/cbf8318315a102e65f288f17fba2322cf9cecda2), ownership resolution in [f6d582cb](https://github.com/router-for-me/CLIProxyAPI/commit/f6d582cb68a46c2a6523d3ed3f58b036096c752c) | Remove the defensive copy immediately discarded when a `data:` line is rebuilt, in both bootstrap and established-stream loops. Comments, event fields and other non-data lines still receive owned storage. Keep bootstrap budgets, error/status propagation, terminal output, usage and replay behavior. Exclude the absent Grok keepalive path. |
| Claude request translation | Claude portion of [639b7f11](https://github.com/router-for-me/CLIProxyAPI/commit/639b7f1126b4c357c28f5016495113e425806e89), pair-helper prerequisite from [8a6a3968](https://github.com/router-for-me/CLIProxyAPI/commit/8a6a39684d11b888644bb1cebc55134856064731) | Reuse translation only when the original and working payloads describe the same bytes of the same backing array and no plugin hooks are installed. Retain separate output buffers, two ordered stateful hook calls, distinct-input handling and API-key compatibility behavior. Apply to Claude Execute/ExecuteStream, preserving native recognition, canonical thinking and subsequent wire processing. Exclude Gemini and the SDK unregister API added for upstream's counting test. |
| Responses stream request selection | Request-selection portion of [8a6a3968](https://github.com/router-for-me/CLIProxyAPI/commit/8a6a39684d11b888644bb1cebc55134856064731) | Choose and validate the fixed original/fallback request once per stream, including caching an invalid/absent result. Keep this in the existing stream state. Preserve original-request precedence, independent stream state and existing namespace/tool-name resolution. Do not import the new tool-index maps or rewrite tool declarations, choices or history. |
| Embedded Codex model catalog | [94b7cc2e](https://github.com/router-for-me/CLIProxyAPI/commit/94b7cc2ee0f0b6bff1a85f21e1977954c31b50fd) | Remove only `gpt-5.3-codex-spark` from embedded `codex-plus` and `codex-pro`, matching the public catalog at [models@0173cfad](https://github.com/router-for-me/models/commit/0173cfadb6a8b4b126db6f7ba16315683219e58b). This aligns startup/offline/local-model fallback advertising with that catalog. The separate Codex client metadata still includes Spark upstream and locally; no client metadata replacement or global model denylist is introduced. |

Claude Code remains **2.1.258**, and Codex remains **0.154.0**, matching v7.3.12. No credentials, cooldown, disabled-state, scheduling, proxy priority, timeout, provider surface or configuration policy changed.

Validation used Go **1.27.1**, macOS arm64, **CGO_ENABLED=1**, isolated source and dedicated temporary caches. The unchanged production baseline passed the new SSE ownership and Responses request-selection/isolation checks; the selected changes are performance improvements rather than newly reproduced protocol failures. The candidate passed focused compatibility regressions, full tests (**67** packages passed, **24** packages had no tests), race tests (**3** packages passed: `internal/runtime/executor`, `internal/runtime/executor/helps`, `internal/translator/openai/openai/responses`), and `go build -o <task-directory>/test-output ./cmd/server`. All commands completed with exit code 0. Formatting, `git diff --check`, exact catalog-delta checks and final diff review passed.

The full and race commands used `-count=1 -json -skip '^TestClaudeStandardRoundTripper(BindsLocalIP|HTTPSProxyKeepsBindAndProxyTLS)$'`; the full command targeted `./...`. The existing macOS limitation was reconfirmed by a local bind attempt to 127.0.0.2 returning `EADDRNOTAVAIL`. The optional `TestClaudeCodeTLSClientHelloCapture` skipped because `CPA_TLS_FP_PROXY` was unset. No other test-level skips or failures occurred in these runs. No Linux test run, live inference request or server operation was performed.

Three repetitions of `-benchmem -benchtime=200ms` produced these medians on the local Apple M4 Max. These are synthetic processing/allocation measurements, not production latency claims:

| Benchmark scope | Before | After | Interpretation |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Codex ExecuteStream, 256 x 1 KiB deltas with SSE fields/comments | About 2.15 MB/call for Claude output; 0.995-0.996 MB/call for Responses output | About 1.85 MB/call for Claude; 0.699-0.700 MB/call for Responses | About 296 KB and 261 allocations removed per call, with bootstrap buffering both off/on; 13.8% / 29.7-29.8% fewer allocated bytes. Timing varied and is not claimed as an improvement. |
| Claude request pair, 1 MiB Responses input, normal/compat mode | 22.19 / 22.17 ms; 25.37 / 23.25 MB | 11.20 / 11.09 ms; 13.74 / 12.68 MB | About half the translation time in the eligible identical-input case; separate-translation and paired paths measured with the same fixture. |
| Responses stream, 100 history turns and 256 chunks | 107.95 ms | 3.95 ms | About 96.3% less processing time in this long-request fixture by avoiding full JSON revalidation per chunk; existing tool-name lookup is retained. |

The tested input manifest SHA-256 is `832997e090ea785846c2b6dbd6cba0dd29f53b8b721d81c0f430a98b3ba6ff5c`. It covers **1544** tracked or non-ignored untracked regular files, excluding only `docs/upstream-sync.md`: sort unique relative paths from `git ls-files -co --exclude-standard -z`, encode each as `<SHA-256 of file bytes><two spaces><relative path>\n`, concatenate and hash. The candidate inputs matched before and after all checks, after local integration and again before publication. The temporary worktree, logs, executable and dedicated build/test caches were removed after integration. Publication reused the successful validation without changing source/tests, rerunning Go checks or regenerating build caches. GitHub's `main` ref was read back as `6b6d721f33ed274a4dc28664eb62b6d152409fc7` before this publication-ledger update.
